## Snapshot
Three reportable segments: **North America** (Q1'26 $104.1B rev, +12%, 7.9% OI margin), **International** ($39.8B, +11% ex-FX), and **AWS** ($37.6B, +28%, $14.2B OI). AWS is ~21% of revenue but the overwhelming majority of operating income and the entire valuation debate. On top of the retail flywheel (1P/3P marketplace, FBA, Prime, fast/quick commerce) sit three high-margin attach businesses — **Advertising** ($17.2B Q1'26, +22%), **Prime Video**, and now an AI-infrastructure complex (Trainium/Graviton custom silicon, Bedrock, Anthropic anchor). New optionality: **Amazon LEO** (satellite broadband, commercial launch Q3'26) and **Amazon Supply Chain Services** (3P logistics / LTL). The stock is positioned by the Street as the "infrastructure backbone of AI" — OpenAI + Anthropic both running on AWS (JPM AI Capex & Financing, 2025-11-10: "best positioned of the hyperscalers to generate tangible returns from AI investment").

## At a glance — product · buyer · supplier
| | |
|---|---|
| **Sells (top 3)** | 1) AWS cloud + custom silicon (Trainium/Graviton) · 2) Retail 1P/3P marketplace, Prime, FBA · 3) Advertising ($17.2B Q1'26, +22%) |
| **Main buyer(s)** | AWS: enterprises + frontier AI labs (Anthropic largest, >$100B / 5GW deal; OpenAI $38B 7-yr); retail: consumers; ads: brands/sellers |
| **Key suppliers** | TSMC (N3P foundry for Trainium), HBM3E from SK Hynix/Micron, Alchip (Trn3/Trn4 ASIC backend), Nvidia (GB200/GB300), own datacenters |

Context for how much of the pipeline is real: the total project pipeline **surpassed 400GW (+23GW m/m to 404GW)** but Bernstein views only **~35%, or 170GW, as ultimately credible** — with **hyperscaler self-builds at the HIGHEST confidence level, 79% credibility (73GW)**, versus colocation at 67% (55GW). **Stranded capacity rose 7GW in August to a record 12% of the total pipeline.**

**🆕 2026-08-27 — THREE AWS READ-THROUGHS FROM SEMI-SIDE SOURCES THIS WEEK, ALL POINTING THE SAME WAY: AMAZON IS DRIVING THE INTEROPERABILITY AGENDA, NOT RESISTING [[NVDA]].**
- 🔴 **THE DEMAND-SIDE EXPLANATION FOR THE 2M-GPU DEAL, FROM MS'S POST-PRINT CALL — AND IT INVERTS THE "AMAZON DOESN'T CARE ABOUT NVIDIA ANYMORE" READ: *"I think it's a **very large deal, maybe LARGER than the 2 million units** they talked about… and I think it is **coming at CUSTOMER REQUEST**. **The FRONTIER MODEL GUYS want to use TRAINIUM, and also want to use VERA RUBIN, and want those things to INTEROPERATE.** And so I think that's where the demand is coming from, and I think **Amazon will SUPPORT THE DEMAND that their frontier labs will have**"* (MS · Joseph Moore, post-print callback, 2026-08-27). He reads NVDA's custom-NVHBM release the same way: *"my guess would be that a lot of the **NVLink Fusion stuff is coming from AMAZON**, who wants **INTEROPERABILITY BETWEEN THE ASIC AND THE GPU**, between Trainium and GPU, and is working hard to get that."*** ➤ **The strategic reading: AWS is not choosing between Trainium and NVIDIA; its largest customers are demanding both in one fabric, and AWS is buying to serve that. That is a better outcome for AWS's capture rate than either a pure-Trainium or a pure-NVDA path, and it explains why Annapurna adopts NVHBM from Trainium4.**
- **CORROBORATION FROM THE SUPPLY CHAIN, WITH A DATE: [[ALAB]] told the Jefferies conference that the *"**NVLink Fusion program with NVDA/AWS may ship LATE NEXT YEAR via a TRANSLATION LAYER**"*, and separately that its **NPO revenue begins in the back half of C27, more material in C28, with the FIRST ROLLOUT TIED TO AWS** (Jefferies, 2026-08-27). ➤ **Two independent marks that AWS is the lead customer for the next optical and interconnect transitions, with C27 dates attached.**
- ⚠️ **AND ONE UNFLATTERING MARK ON THE OTHER SIDE OF THE LEDGER, ALREADY TRACKED ON [[MRVL]]: SIG has **Trainium 2 spot pricing FLAT q/q and available in only ~1% of AWS regions**, with **Trainium 3 spot instances not yet launched.** ➤ Held here because it is the direct counterweight to the interoperability story: the demand may be real, but the deployed Trainium footprint is still thin.**
- **A smaller optical read-through from the same conference: STMicro said it is engaged with **80% of the optical market**, with **AWS <30%** penetrated (vs China <50%, rest US) — i.e. AWS is the least-penetrated large optical account in ST's book, and its **SiPho mix is expected to go from 5% to 30% by 2028** with **NPO first shipments 2H27** at **2x transceiver content** (Jefferies, 2026-08-27).**

**🆕🔴🔴🔴 2026-08-26 — AWS TRIPLES DOWN ON [[NVDA]] ACROSS THE *FULL STACK*, AND ANNAPURNA ADOPTS NVIDIA'S NEW MEMORY STANDARD FROM TRAINIUM4. THIS IS THE MOST CONSEQUENTIAL SILICON-STRATEGY DATAPOINT ON THIS PAGE THIS YEAR, AND IT CUTS AGAINST THE "TRAINIUM DISPLACES GPU" THESIS.**
- **The announcement: *"**2 million ADDITIONAL NVIDIA GPUs** across AWS's global infrastructure"* (Blackwell Ultra + Rubin + Rubin Ultra) in **2027-2028** — MS Asia dates it *"starting this quarter through 2Q FY29 (~2QCY28)"* — **in ADDITION to the GTC commitment of >1M GPUs in 2026, because *"demand has exceeded those expectations."*** Plus **NVIDIA Vera CPU coming to AWS for agentic AI**, and **NVLink Fusion with NVHBM**. NVDA's framing: *"expanding the partnership across the **FULL STACK** — GPUs, CPUs, networking, open models and software"* (NVIDIA press release + @nvidianewsroom, corpus; MS TMT · Wigg; MS Asia · Amerian, 2026-08-26).
- 🔴🔴 **THE ANNAPURNA LINE IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS: *"[[AMZN]]'s **Annapurna Labs will be the FIRST to work on NVHBM** as part of its broader collaboration with NVIDIA around NVLink Fusion. Annapurna Labs will support NVLink Fusion with its **next-generation Trainium chips STARTING WITH TRAINIUM4**, which would allow **Amazon chips and NVIDIA GPUs to work together with COMMON RACK-SCALE ARCHITECTURE**."*** NVHBM integrates NVDA's custom memory controller into the HBM base die (claimed **+30% bandwidth, −15% HBM power, +25% free XPU die area vs standard HBM4E**), established as a **standard *"available from multiple memory providers"*** (NVIDIA blog, relayed by Jefferies · Conor O'Mara, 2026-08-26).
➤ 🔴 **READ IT AS A STRATEGY CHOICE, NOT A PURCHASE ORDER: Amazon is converging Trainium onto NVDA's interconnect *and* NVDA's memory interface, on a common rack architecture. That lowers Amazon's own integration cost and raises optionality between its silicon and NVDA's — but it also means the differentiation of Trainium narrows to the compute die, and NVDA collects attach revenue on Amazon's ASIC programme. Redburn: *"Fears that ASICs significantly erode NVDA earnings power or market position should be **MATERIALLY CALMED**… an established ASIC player is increasing reliance on Nvidia full-stack silicon"* (2026-08-27). MS: *"bullish relative to concerns of **AMZN UNDER-INVESTING in NVDA given AWS Trainium success**."* See [[NVDA]], [themes/custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).**
- ⚠️⚠️ **THE DEAL'S SIZE IS DISPUTED BY 3.3x: SIG calls the 2M GPUs *"likely providing at least **~$60BN** of revenue upside"* (SIG · Rolland, 2026-08-27); Rothschild & Co Redburn calls it *"the **$200BN** AWS deal"* / *"AWS $200bn order"* (2026-08-27). Neither shows arithmetic. Do not adopt either.**
- 🔴 **AND THE COUNTER-EVIDENCE ON TRAINIUM'S ACTUAL DEPLOYMENT, WHICH IS UNCOMFORTABLE: SIG's cloud-instance tracker has **Trainium 2 available in only ~1% of AWS regions**, with **spot pricing FLAT q/q at an index of 24** (*"roughly in line with the 'normal' historical average of ~25"*) — against AWS B200 in **~16%** of regions, H100 **~32%**, H200 **~29%**, and B300 newly in **~11%**. **Trainium 3 spot instances have not launched.** SIG's verdict on the ASIC line: *"Modest positive for [[AVGO]], **Neutral for [[MRVL]]**"* (SIG · Rolland, 2026-08-26). ➤ **~1% region availability and flat pricing is hard to reconcile with the "Trainium success" premise the NVDA deal is said to allay. Both readings now sit on this page; the region series is the more falsifiable.**

See [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md).**

- **AND A CHINESE OPEN-WEIGHT MODEL MAY BE COMING TO AWS ON A REVENUE SHARE: *"Reuters reports **Moonshot is negotiating revenue-sharing agreements with [[MSFT]], Amazon and [[GOOG]] to host its Kimi K3 model** on Azure, AWS and Google Cloud, seeking **up to a 30% share of K3-related services revenue**… Any deal would be **the FIRST big revenue-sharing pact between a Chinese AI firm and a major US cloud provider**"* — advancing *"despite acute US-China tensions, chip export bans and direct political heat on Moonshot, with Treasury Secretary Bessent having threatened to add it to a trade blacklist and US officials accusing it of distilling [[ANTHROPIC]]'s Fable."* *"Few customers will self-host a **2.8 trillion-parameter** model given the compute cost, so hosting plus revenue share is how an open-weight developer monetises."* **Talks are early; revenue split, data access and token-usage auditing unresolved; Alibaba reportedly pursuing similar arrangements** (Rothschild & Co Redburn, 2026-08-27). See [themes/china-export](themes/china-export.md).**
- **A structural datapoint on the retail side from the same week: *"AI-driven changes to [[GOOG]] search are helping make Amazon an **increasingly essential sales outlet for independent merchants**"* (@theinformation, corpus, 2026-08-26).**

**🆕🔴🔴 2026-08-26 — NVIDIA ANNOUNCED AN AWS EXPANSION ON ITS OWN EARNINGS CALL, AND IT IS THE LARGEST NAMED GPU COMMITMENT AWS HAS EVER HAD ATTACHED TO IT: AN ADDITIONAL 2 MILLION GPUs, DEPLOYING FROM THIS QUARTER THROUGH Q2 OF NVIDIA'S FISCAL 2029.** (NVIDIA Q2 FY27 earnings call + press release, both 2026-08-26 — **company primary, but the COUNTERPARTY's disclosure, not Amazon's. No AMZN rating, PT or estimate attached.**) Colette Kress: *"Today, we are delighted to announce an expansion of our partnership with AWS. **Building on its already vast installed base of NVIDIA compute, AWS is deploying an ADDITIONAL 2 MILLION GPUs starting this quarter through the second quarter of fiscal 29, along with VERA CPUs, some integrated with Rubin.** … AWS will serve NVIDIA Llama Nemotron family of open models on Amazon Bedrock and SageMaker. **Amazon will also adopt our full physical AI stack — Omniverse, Cosmos, Isaac and Jetson — to power its fleet of WAREHOUSE ROBOTS.**"* Separately, in the CPU section: Vera *"shipments already underway to our lead partners including OCI, SpaceX, and **starting this quarter, AWS**."*

🔴 **WHY THIS MATTERS MORE THAN A NORMAL VENDOR ANNOUNCEMENT, AND IT CUTS BOTH WAYS ON THIS PAGE'S CENTRAL DEBATE.** This page's whole custom-silicon thread — Trainium economics, the Bedrock token-mix disclosure from Amazon IR via BTG (08-19: the MAJORITY of Bedrock tokens already run on Trainium), the "$3bn/GW, ~2.6GW/annum CY27-28" Trainium sizing carried from the Wells Fargo MRVL note — argues AWS is structurally reducing its NVIDIA dependence. **A 2mn-GPU incremental commitment over roughly eight quarters, plus adoption of Vera CPUs, plus putting NVIDIA's open models on Bedrock/SageMaker, is the opposite vector.** ➜ **The honest reading is that these are not mutually exclusive: AWS is scaling Trainium for internal/Bedrock inference AND buying merchant NVIDIA capacity for the demand it cannot serve, which is exactly what Amazon IR described on 08-19 when it said the ~3-year payback figure was blended *"across Trainium AND GPUs… across both AI and core services."* **Nothing on this page is superseded; the custom-silicon thesis now has to coexist with the largest named NVIDIA order AWS has disclosed.**

⚠️⚠️ **FOUR THINGS THAT ARE NOT DISCLOSED, AND THEY ARE ALL THE THINGS A MODEL NEEDS: (1) NO DOLLAR VALUE — 2mn GPUs of unspecified mix (Blackwell? Vera Rubin? both?) at unspecified ASPs; (2) NO SPLIT between GPU generations, and NVIDIA's own guide has Vera Rubin at only ~20% of FQ3 DC revenue, so the front of this deployment is largely Blackwell-class; (3) the window is stated in NVIDIA'S FISCAL CALENDAR — "this quarter through the second quarter of fiscal 29" is NVIDIA FQ3 FY27 (Aug-Oct 2026) through NVIDIA FQ2 FY29 (May-Jul 2028), i.e. ~8 quarters, NOT Amazon's fiscal calendar; (4) NO INCREMENTALITY TEST — "additional" is measured against an undisclosed installed base, and neither company has said whether this was already inside AWS's capex guide. **Do not convert 2mn GPUs into an AWS capex delta without those four.**

✅ **THE PHYSICAL-AI ITEM IS SMALL IN DOLLARS BUT IT IS THE ONE THAT TOUCHES THE RETAIL THESIS ON THIS PAGE: Amazon adopting Omniverse/Cosmos/Isaac/Jetson for its warehouse-robot fleet sits directly on top of the BofA "Project Tetromino" work already logged here (08-24: ~$530mn by 2029, up to 2.5x throughput per delivery station, against a cost-to-serve that has fallen 25% in 4.5 years to $4.33/unit). It is the software/simulation layer under the same automation programme.** ⚠️ No dollars, no timeline and no unit count disclosed for the robotics stack.

- 🔴 **Trainium units — the check is moving up:** *"On AMZN, **Trn checks continue to sound strong — hearing original expectation around 1M for 2H which is being revised UP, and Trn 2 + Trn 3 now around close to 2.7M**"* (TMTB EOD Wrap, 2026-08-25). ⚠️ **Buy-side desk channel figure, no source named, and the period basis of the 2.7M ("Trn2 + Trn3") is not stated — do not treat it as a calendar-year unit forecast.**
- **From the supply chain:** MS **doubled Alchip's 2028 Trainium4 revenue estimate to US$8bn from US$4bn**, assuming **≥2.5M Trainium4 units and ~30% Alchip allocation** (MS · Charlie Chan, 2026-08-25); [[MRVL]]'s covering analyst has **Trainium growing 25-30% in C26 with incremental Trainium 4 content from Celestial and a potential Trainium 5 volume ramp in C28** (Jefferies · Blayne Curtis, 2026-08-25).

**NEW 2026-08-18 — "THE ANSWER IS ALWAYS AMAZON": SELF-AS-FIRST-CUSTOMER IS THE MOAT.** Ben Thompson's mechanism is not spare capacity; it is that Amazon's own scale lets it incubate services and hardware, hide weak early Graviton/Trainium generations beneath managed services, iterate them with captive volume, then sell the mature product externally. Retail/logistics are difficult for model-layer AI to attack and supply a recurring test bed for call-center, agent and infrastructure products. Together with [[GOOG]], Amazon is the most credible long-run [[NVDA]] challenger because Trainium can be sold as commodity silicon from the industry's lowest-cost capital base (Ben Thompson, *Invest Like the Best*, 2026-08-18).

**🆕🔴 2026-08-14 — THE EC2 PRICE-HIKE HYPOTHESIS NOW HAS CUSTOMER-SIDE CONFIRMATION, AND IT ARRIVES WITH THE CONTRACT MECHANICS THAT DETERMINE HOW FAST IT REACHES REVENUE.** (Capstone-hosted expert call · **Daniel Abraahão**, cloud specialist / ex-Banco Inter practitioner, 2026-08-14 — call in Portuguese; summary is Capstone's own. ⚠️ **A single practitioner with buyer-side visibility into one large Brazilian bank's AWS estate. NOT an AWS disclosure, NOT sell-side, and the revenue-split percentages below are explicitly his estimates.**)
- 🔴 **THE VALUE IS THAT IT CORROBORATES AN EXISTING SELL-SIDE HYPOTHESIS FROM THE OTHER SIDE OF THE TABLE, AND MATCHES ITS KEY INPUT EXACTLY.** GS modelled *"a potential **20% EC2 price hike in 2H26 = ~$2-4B net rev tailwind (EC2 ~50%+ of AWS)"*** back on 2026-07-07 (Sheridan preview, already on this page). The expert, unprompted and from the buyer's chair, independently puts **EC2 at ~50% of AWS core revenue** and says AWS is **considering and applying price increases on EC2 and on GPUs**, driven by **hardware supply-chain scarcity** — behaviour he frames as running *"against the culture"* of a company whose entire pricing history is cuts. ➜ **Two independent sources, one an analyst model and one a customer, agree on both the ~50% EC2 weighting and the direction of price. That is the strongest form this datapoint has taken on the page.**
- ⚠️ **Note what is NOT corroborated: the 20% magnitude. The expert confirms direction and product scope (EC2 *and* GPU, i.e. broader than accelerators), not the size of the increase.**
- **The contract architecture, which is the useful part and is checkable over time:** **~80–90% of AWS ACCOUNTS are on-demand (long tail), but only ~40% of FINANCIAL VOLUME is** — **~60% of AWS revenue volume sits under PPAs (Private Pricing Agreements, formerly EDPs) or Reserved Instances.** AWS **prefers 3-year PPAs and discourages 5- or 7-year terms** precisely to preserve renegotiation flexibility. ➜ **This is the mechanism that determines how fast the price increases above actually reach revenue: with ~60% of dollars locked for up to three years, a list-price rise is a slow-moving mix effect, not a step-change — and it also means AWS is structurally protected on the way DOWN.**
- **Revenue composition (expert estimates, not disclosed):** **EC2 ≈ 50% of AWS core revenue**; **databases (RDS/Aurora/DynamoDB/S3) ≈ 20–30%**; **emerging services including AI (Bedrock, SageMaker) ≈ 20%**. Higher-level AI/ML services **run on EC2 underneath**, generating indirect EC2 revenue. ⚠️ **These do not tie to any Amazon disclosure — AWS does not break out service-level revenue. Use as a shape, not as a number.**
- **On Bedrock and the competitive frame against the labs:** Bedrock positioned as a **marketplace/ecosystem carrying ~90% of the market's models** ([[ANTHROPIC]], [[OPENAI]], Meta, open-source), with the differentiator being **security and regulated-industry experience — "20+ years, 'Security is Job Zero'" — versus Anthropic and OpenAI, whom the expert calls "babies" in that dimension.** ➜ **Same conclusion Brian Nowak reaches from the sell-side the same day (see below), arrived at from the buyer's chair rather than the model's.**
- ⚠️ **Name spelling: the source renders the expert as "Daniel Abraahao" (the Capstone file name); the surname is almost certainly "Abrahão." Recorded as it appears in the source.**

**🆕 2026-08-14 — MS's GenAI-ROIC pod. ⚠️ MOSTLY A RELAY: this is Nowak restating the 07-27 "Paths to 25-50% GenAI ROIC" and the 08-12 open-weight follow-up, BOTH of which are already folded on this page in full. Only the mechanism detail below is incremental — do not count it as a second, independent confirmation of the same view.** (Morgan Stanley · **Brian Nowak**, GenAI-ROIC pod on AMZN / GOOGL / META, via Capstone desk summary + MS Tech Talk, 2026-08-14. No rating, PT or estimate change.)
- **Incremental ①, the mechanism under the ROIC number:** the governing metric is **tokens per second per GPU**, driven by three levers — **hardware (HBM capacity: H100 80GB → Blackwell Ultra / Rubin 288GB), model architecture (MoE sparsity), and software (batching)** — with a **45%+ ROIC** scenario for both first-party model developers and AIaaS hyperscalers. ➜ **The batching lever is the one that matters for AMZN and it is a structural argument, not a technology one: "hyperscalers have an advantage here due to their DEEPER ENTERPRISE FOOTPRINTS providing steady, high volumes of concurrent traffic, allowing them to KEEP BATCHES FULL." Utilisation, not silicon, is the moat — which is a different claim from "compute is the scarce resource" already on the page, and a more falsifiable one.**
- **Incremental ②, AMZN-specific:** **Bedrock called a genuine differentiator** on model choice, token minimisation and data residency/security *"especially against Asian models"*, with **Trainium adding efficiency**. **What Nowak says to monitor: Trainium 3/4 performance evidence and Bedrock strategy updates at re:Invent.** ➜ **Independently echoed by the buyer-side expert above, who reaches the same security/regulated-industry conclusion from the customer's chair — and puts Bedrock's model catalogue at ~90% of the market's models.**
- ⚠️ **Transcription artefact in the summary: DeepSeek is rendered "Deep Sea" in the MoE example ("670bn total / 37bn active parameters, ~5% sparsity"). Corrected here.**
- **Third-party corroboration on Trainium the same week, and it is mixed:** **Trainium2 SPOT pricing rose +18% MoM in July, the biggest move in the accelerator tracker** — but the same tracker concludes **"Trainium and Inferentia appear LESS COMPETITIVE on raw compute economics"** on a $/hr/PFLOP basis, with **TPU the closest alternative to NVDA and Trainium behind it** (UBS Evidence Lab / Timothy Arcuri, "Cloud Accelerator Study — Aug'26," 2026-08-14). ➜ **Rising price on a chip that screens poorly on raw economics is a scarcity signal, not a performance signal.**
- **And from the ASIC designer's own call:** Alchip says **Trainium3 "would ramp stably" in 2H26**, that its N3 accelerator for the North-America CSP customer **started shipping in May** with production ramping through 3Q26, and that **"customers are increasing the ASIC usage in AI data center — 3-4x cost saving compared to current GPU"** (MS · Charlie Chan, Alchip 2Q26 analyst-meeting note, 2026-08-14/15 — see [AVGO.md](AVGO.md)/[MRVL.md](MRVL.md)). ⚠️ **"3-4x cost saving vs current GPU" is the ASIC vendor's own framing, unbenchmarked and almost certainly not TCO-adjusted. Logged, not adopted.**

**🆕 2026-08-10 — Jassy's Q2'26 call, the parts that weren't the capex defence (AMZN Q2 2026 call, via Stratechery / Ben Thompson, 2026-08-10).**
- **The chips business is now a disclosed scale number:** *"it's over **$25 billion in annual revenue** at this point"* for Trainium + Graviton, with **"multiyear, multi-gigawatt commitments from the 2 largest AI labs, Anthropic and OpenAI,"** and *"an incredible amount of demand for Trainium."* Note Amazon currently classifies Trainium as its own business line even though **it is all internal sales** — Jassy's answer to the "will you sell Trainium" question points at that changing ("we do have an increasing number of customers who are…"). Cross-ref [ANTHROPIC.md](ANTHROPIC.md), [OPENAI.md](OPENAI.md), [NVDA.md](NVDA.md).
- **Why AI helps the non-AI business — three mechanisms, stated:** (1) proximity — *"they want their AI inference to reside near their other applications and data, and more of it resides in AWS than anywhere else"*; (2) **post-training RL and agent tool use run mostly on CPUs, not accelerators**, and Jassy claims **Graviton delivers 30-40% better price/performance** than alternatives, plus storage and vector databases; (3) CFO Brian Olsavsky: *"we see a strong linkage between AI spend and core growth… We expect this relationship to strengthen over time."*
- **The barbell framing (the most useful new mental model on the call):** at one end **the AI labs consuming "gobs and gobs of compute" plus a few runaway apps like Claude Code and ChatGPT**; at the other **enterprises getting real value from cost avoidance and productivity** (customer service, BPA, fraud); **in the middle, all current enterprise production workloads, most of which are not yet using inference pervasively** — which Jassy expects to become **"the largest absolute segment."** He explicitly declines to claim the middle ramps at the same slope as the ends. AWS *"has the potential to be a $1 trillion revenue business."*
- **Frontier models — the internally inconsistent answer:** Jassy first argues *"AWS and Amazon can have a wildly successful business without its own frontier model… there is not going to be one model to rule the world,"* then says Amazon **is** pursuing one, for cost control, prioritisation and speed, expecting *"within the next few years… at least a half dozen models that are comparably good to each other. They'll all be in Bedrock, and one of them will be ours."* Thompson's verdict is worth carrying as the skeptical marker: *"I don't think there is any universe where Amazon is making anything competitive with Anthropic and OpenAI; let's just say I found the previous answer about how AWS doesn't need to be on the frontier much more believable."*
- **Operational tell:** **"AWS Tells Engineers to Cut CPU Waste Amid Crunch"** (The Information, week of 2026-08-09) — consistent with the CPU-side tightness Jassy describes as a *demand* story.

**Q2 FY26 (print + call 2026-07-30, AMC) — beat on everything that matters, paid for in cash.** Revenue **$200.6B, +20% y/y** (Street $197B; guide $194-199B); **operating income $27.5B** vs Street $23.6B and vs its own guide of **$20-24B**. **AWS +36.7% y/y (37% FX-neutral) — fastest growth in 18 quarters** — vs a buy-side bogey of ~35% and >500bp above consensus; **AWS operating margin 39.4%**, ~600bp above plan and up from ~38% in Q1. AWS added ~$4.7B sequentially. **AWS backlog $496B** (vs MS $474B, from $364B at Q1). Two new scaling disclosures: **the AWS AI business exceeded a >$25B annual run-rate and the chips business separately exceeded a >$25B run-rate, both growing triple-digit % y/y** — AI run-rate therefore stepped from ~$15B to ~$25B q/q. Advertising accelerated 400bp to **+26%** (vs +22% in Q1); North America segment **+16%** (from +12%), helped by Prime Day timing. **Q3 guide: revenue $195-202B (mid $199.5B) vs Street ~$204B** — a headline miss that is Prime-Day-timing optical (adjusting back, growth would look like $204-209B, ~400bp better — UBS Dwyer); **Q3 op income $22.5-26.5B (mid $24.5B)** vs Street $25B, high end $26.5B above the buy-side ~$25.5-26B bogey. **Cash: OCF $45.3B less $54.2B of property & equipment → FCF −$9B, versus consensus −$1B.** **FY26 capex guide raised to ~$220B from ~$200B, with the increment attributed explicitly to higher memory costs**; "heavy capex over next few years." On the call management gave the season's most specific AI-ROI framework: data-center capital is spent ~2 years ahead of monetization, servers/networking are bought only months ahead and only against visible demand, **it takes "a little less than three years to break even"** on server/networking spend against a **≥5-6 year useful life with most AI capacity contracted for ≥5-year terms**, and the 30+ year data-center shell should yield **"at least five to six generations of server economics"** without repeating the upfront spend. Management also said AWS demand outstrips supply **for 2026 and 2027**, that 2028 demand already visible is "striking," and that AWS "can reach a trillion dollars" of revenue over time. Stock +8-10% AH; the supply chain rallied with it (ALAB, MRVL, CRDO +3-4%). (AMZN Q2 FY26 call, 2026-07-30; Vital Knowledge 2026-07-30; MS Wigg 2026-07-30; GS Sheridan 2026-07-30; UBS Dwyer 2026-07-30; TMT Breakout 2026-07-30.)

**★ ADDENDUM FROM THE PRIMARY TRANSCRIPT (AMZN Q2 2026 earnings call, 2026-07-30 — Jassy / Olsavsky / Fildes; analysts Anmuth-JPM, Post-BofA, Nowak-MS, Sebastian-Baird, Sheridan-GS, Ken-Wells Fargo).** The desk flow above is accurate on the headlines; the transcript adds five things it does not carry, one of which changes how the margin beat should be read.
- **⚠ QUALITY OF THE BEAT: ~$1.2bn of the $27.5bn operating income is DISCRETE, and it lands on both sides of the beat.** Olsavsky: OI "includes the benefit from two items that reduced expenses by approximately **$1.2 billion** during the quarter. First, we received **tariff-related refunds of approximately $600 million** — included in our **North America** segment and representing a significant majority of refunds we expect to receive. Second, we recorded a separate benefit of approximately **$600 million related to the change in fair-value measurement of energy contracts subject to derivative accounting. This primarily impacts the AWS segment.**" These are electricity-supply contracts whose terms make them derivatives; the marks "have not been significant in prior quarters." **So against the $27.5bn reported vs Street $23.6bn, roughly a third of the beat is non-operating, and ~$600mn of it sits inside the AWS margin everyone is quoting.**
- **★ The clean AWS margin number, which no desk note on this page carries: AWS operating income was $16.6bn on $42.2bn of revenue, and margins were "up 650 basis points year-over-year, 520 basis points if you exclude the derivative accounting gain."** So the durable y/y expansion is **~520bps, not ~650bps**, and ~130bps of the reported expansion is a fair-value mark on energy contracts. Olsavsky's own framing of the rest: "the profitability you're seeing from AWS isn't random. It's a result of disciplined efficiency gains, **capacity optimization, which we benefited quite a bit from in Q2**, and always closely managing our fixed costs" — with the standing caveat that "these margins will fluctuate." Jassy adds the structural claim: "we see the margins and returns in AI tracking what we saw with core at the same point of evolution, **actually a little ahead**." **Reconciliation note: this page and the desk notes carry AWS margin at 39.4% and "~600bp above plan"; $16.6bn / $42.2bn = 39.3%, and the y/y expansion decomposes 650bps reported / 520bps clean. Both stay on the page with their bases named.**
- **★ COST PASS-THROUGH — Amazon's answer to the same question Microsoft's IR was asked the same day, and the answers rhyme.** Asked how AWS pricing incorporates future cost inflation given long-term contracts, Jassy: "most of the deals that you sign… tend to be deals and agreements that you've signed. And the deals that you've signed, those will be the prices… over the duration of that contract. **And new agreements that you sign, you always take into account what your costs are in how you ultimately build a price** that you agree to with your customers. And I think it's no secret right now to any company in the world that **there are inflated prices right now on some of the components like memory and hard drives and SSDs.**" **Read against [MSFT](MSFT.md): Microsoft's IR said pricing is NOT locked over a contract's life (frontier-lab pricing resets by capacity tranche; enterprise discount-off-list can move). Amazon's answer is the more conservative version — signed price holds for the signed term, cost inflation is recovered on NEW agreements. Same direction, weaker mechanism, and it implies AWS carries more of the memory-inflation risk inside the existing book than Azure claims to.** This is a genuine cross-company divergence worth tracking as the FY27 tranches/renewals land.
- **★ TRAINIUM MAY BE SOLD AS MERCHANT SILICON.** Asked (Nowak) about selling Trainium to third-party data centers: "we do have **an increasing number of customers who are interested in us providing the training chips to them separate from even from our cloud**, and we're actively having those conversations and exploring, and **I expect there's a real chance we'll do that in the future.**" That converts GS's "$50B if sold to 3Ps" hypothetical (07-07, on this page) into a management-stated live commercial conversation. Demand context given in the same answer: "multiyear, multi-gigawatt commitments from the two largest AI labs in [[ANTHROPIC]] and [[OPENAI]]… we just have an incredible amount of demand for Trainium." New named adopters beyond the labs: **[[UBER]] and Pinterest**, plus startups **Neurobotics, Odyssey, 12 Labs, Descartes, Poolside, Karakuri, Neto AI and Splash Music**.
- **★ AMAZON IS BUILDING ITS OWN FRONTIER MODEL — and framed it as a cost lever, not a capability race.** Jassy: "AWS and Amazon can have a wildly successful business **without** its own frontier model… there is not going to be one model to rule the world." But: "**we are pursuing our own frontier model**… first of which is it just gives us additional control over cost, cost for our own consumer applications, but also we're trying to drive cost down for customers… **within the next few years you're going to have at least a half-dozen models that are comparably good to each other — they'll all be in Bedrock and one of them will be ours.**" A structural read-through for [[ANTHROPIC]] and [[OPENAI]]: their largest infrastructure partner intends to be a same-shelf competitor, using its own model as the cheap tier — the same MAI-as-cheap-tier dynamic already documented on [MSFT](MSFT.md).
- **Capacity and the demand runway, in management's words:** capex goes to "**approximately $220 billion** in cash CapEx in 2026, **the higher cost of memory pushing this number up from our prior estimate of about $200 billion. But even at that amount, we will still not have enough capacity to meet all the demand we have in 2026, and I believe this dynamic will also be true in 2027 too. In fact, the demand we already have for 2028 is striking.**" On the physical build: "we're on pace with the capacity build that we talked about a few quarters ago where we said we expect to have **double the power capacity by the end of '27 that we had in '25**"; and on reservation, "apart from what we've talked about in '26, **the lion's share of capacity in '27… is largely reserved, and we have quite a bit of capacity that's already been reserved for '28**." Long-run: "we long believed AWS could become a few $100 billion revenue business and **now believe it will be at least double that and very possibly be a $1 trillion annual revenue business.**"
- **Segment and scale marks from the call (several not on this page):** AWS annualized run-rate **$169bn**; AWS added "**over $4.6 billion in revenue quarter-over-quarter, about 80% more than our largest increase ever**" (the desk notes say ~$4.7bn — the primary figure is $4.6bn); revenue growth "**accelerating for the fifth straight quarter, our fastest growth in 18 quarters back when AWS was less than half its current revenue size**"; backlog **$496bn growing triple-digits**, and separately **"RPO reported is 2.5 times that of the third quarter of '25"** (analyst framing, unchallenged). **North America revenue $116.2bn +16% with OI $9.1bn / 7.9% margin; International $42.2bn +15% ex-FX with OI $1.7bn / 4.1% margin; worldwide paid units +17%; advertising $19.8bn +26%; Q2 cash capex $53.1bn.** Graviton: "**used by 98% of our top 1,000 EC2 customers**," revenue commitments "**increased nearly three times quarter-over-quarter**," and "**Graviton 5 is growing nearly 2x faster than Graviton 4 did**," at 30-40% better price-performance. Kiro (agentic coding) "**up to 50% more cost-effective than others and tripled in usage quarter-over-quarter**." Q3 guide mechanics: Prime Day timing worth "**nearly 400 basis points**" of the Q2→Q3 deceleration, plus "**approximately 80 basis points**" of FX headwind.
- **The demand curve, restated (the "barbell," now with a middle):** "the adoption curve in AI right now is very barbelled. There is on one end… **the AI labs are consuming gobs and gobs of compute and there are a few runaway successful generative AI applications like Claude Code and ChatGPT**. And on the other end… **enterprises who are getting real value from AI in cost avoidance and productivity**… In the middle of the barbell is all of the current enterprise production workloads, some of which are using inference in a pervasive way, **but most of which aren't. And that is going to change very significantly over time… that will be the largest absolute segment.**" With the anchor: "**85% of the global IT spend is still on-premises.**" Also: post-training RL and agent tool use run "mostly… on CPUs versus AI accelerators," which is why "growth in AI drives core."
- ⚠️ **SOURCE CAVEAT: this is the Bloomberg LIVE (machine-generated) transcript, and it is visibly imperfect on proper nouns** — Trainium appears as "Tradium/Tranium," Graviton as "Gravitat," Kiro as "curo," Amazon Quick as "Quake," Claude Code as "Cloud Code," Anthropic as "anthropic/Anthropic," Profitero as "Profittero," Prime Day as "Prime Bay." Bloomberg's own disclaimer: "This transcript may not be 100 percent accurate and may contain misspellings." **All figures quoted above were checked as internally consistent (segment revenues sum to $200.6bn; $16.6bn/$42.2bn ties to the ~39% margin; $600mn + $600mn ties to the $1.2bn) — but treat product-name spellings as normalised by us, not as printed.** (AMZN Q2 2026 earnings call, 2026-07-30) [Transcript](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/amzn_earnigns_call.html)

**Q1 FY26 (call 2026-04-29):** Revenue $181.5B, +17% y/y (+15% ex-FX); operating income $23.9B; **operating margin 13.1% — highest ever**. AWS grew **28% y/y, fastest in 15 quarters**, +$2B q/q (largest Q4→Q1 step-up ever), $150B run-rate. **AWS backlog $364B** (Jassy, Q1) — and explicitly **excludes the >$100B Anthropic deal** announced subsequently. AI revenue run-rate >$15B; Bedrock customer spend +170% q/q, 125k+ customers, ~80% of the Fortune 100; OpenAI's GPT-5.4 added to Bedrock (5.5 imminent). **Custom silicon >$20B run-rate, +40% q/q; Trainium revenue commitments exceed $225B**; Trainium3 "nearly fully subscribed." Retail unit growth +15% (highest since COVID); grocery now #2 US grocer ($150B+ gross sales '25). **Q1 cash capex $43.2B**; FY26 capex guide ~$200B (Q4'25 call), predominantly AWS.

- **JPM "AI Capex 2.0" (2026-06-16):** corroborates the AWS reacceleration — 1Q cloud +28% on a **$150bn run-rate**, +$2bn q/q (largest 4Q→1Q AWS step-up ever), and a **$364bn backlog that excludes the >$100bn Anthropic expansion**. Custom silicon now central: **>$225bn of Trainium revenue commitments from Anthropic + OpenAI**, with mgmt arguing the program saves "tens of billions in annual capex." Funding math: FY26 capex **~$200bn → ~$300bn FY27E → ~$350bn FY28E**; TTM FCF already collapsed to **~$1bn (from ~$26bn a year ago)**, leaving a cumulative ~$97bn funding gap FY26-28 before the OpenAI/Anthropic facilities. AMZN responded with **~$67bn of bond issuance across four currencies in 1H** plus a new **$17.5bn three-year delayed-draw term loan (Sept-30 commitment expiry)** — ~$85bn of new funding capacity arranged in <6 months without touching equity (against a ~$33bn FY26 gap), which JPM reads as pre-funding the FY27 ramp; ~$143bn cash on hand. Model-lab entanglement: $15bn OpenAI stake + a further $35bn equity commitment letter, plus Anthropic investments that could total ~$33bn and generated ~$17bn of MtM gains in 1Q (JPM "AI Capex 2.0", 2026-06-16).
- **OpenAI/Anthropic compute deals on AWS (JPM "AI Capex 2.0" capacity table, 2026-06-16):** OpenAI–AWS = **$38bn 7-yr** (Nov-25, NVIDIA GB200/GB300) **plus a ~$100bn+ / ~2 GW Trainium deal (Feb-26)** starting 2027; Anthropic–AWS = **up to 5 GW / $100bn+ over 10 years** (Trainium + Graviton). Anthropic trains/serves Claude on **>1M Trainium2 chips (Project Rainier)** and has committed to run Claude on Trainium "for the next decade" — JPM's strongest third-party endorsement of ASIC economics; AWS positions Trainium3 UltraServers at **~30% better TCO per FP8 PFLOP vs Nvidia GB300 NVL72**.
- **Logistics/fulfillment buildout — still in investment, not harvest (Jefferies/MWPVL, 2026-04-09):** MWPVL's Mark frames AMZN as past the bottom of the excess-capacity valley and re-entering an industrial-real-estate **investment cycle — square footage added to the global supply chain in 2027 will be ~35% higher than 2026**. ~1/3 of two-year retail capex is US robotics fulfillment centers; new layer of **six mega-hub sortation centers (~$1.5B total, 80–100k packages/hr)** positions AMZN as a full air/ground/LTL package competitor, targeting a **fully Amazon-Logistics-controlled network by ~2030** (unwinding UPS/USPS). ~6.7B AMZN Logistics packages in 2024; grocery $150B+ gross sales 2025 (#2 US grocer) but online-grocery margins skeptical; pharmacy rolling into the 86 sub-same-day centers (1–3hr Rx delivery). Shift to **ownership over leasing** (190 new facilities in pipeline, 118 owned); 1M+ robots built in-house. Key buy-side caution: automation is ~14–15yr payback / near-breakeven — there is **"no boogie man" margin offset** to the AWS investment cycle on the retail side (Jefferies/MWPVL, 2026-04-09). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_09_jeff_mwpvl_9_apr_26.html)
- **Advertising / digital-ad checks (rich, 2026-06-22):** AMZN ad spend is the standout in UBS's Scott Linzer agency checks — **Q2 +4%, Q3 ~7%, Q4 +7.5–8%, FY ~6–7%**, a meaningful step-up across the board; **Amazon DSP is the explicit call-out**, taking budget from The Trade Desk (now at parity/surpassing on data + ROI), with sponsored products "still working" and new Prime Video/streaming inventory (Netflix/Roku/Disney) inside DSP. Tariff refunds to retailer clients are being "pocketed by Mr. CFO," not yet reallocated to ads — a potential 2H incremental lever (UBS "Scott Linzer ad checks", 2026-06-22). UBS Ben Legg corroborates the mix: Amazon ad FY ~27% (sponsored products 65% of spend, softening to ~+16% May vs +21% April — possible agentic-search headwind + maxed ad loads + merchant-margin squeeze) but **video ads +83% y/y and DSP +77% y/y, both accelerating**; Amazon has "declared war" on Trade Desk (20% vs 1% take rate) (UBS Ben Legg, 2026-06-17). 3P-seller channel checks: new fees pressuring sellers — 3.5% fuel surcharge + can no longer use credit cards to pay for ads; Prime Day pulled earlier (June); Rufus sponsored ads live but advertisers have "zero control" / minimal volume; Q1'26 ad budgets strong, no compression (Jefferies 3P-seller expert, 2026-04-09). [Sources](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_22_scott_linzer_ubs_adv_call_22_jun_26.html) · [Ben Legg](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_17_ben_legg_ubs_17_jun_26.html) · [3P seller](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_09_amzn_jeff_9_apr_26.html)
- **Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter" (2026-06-22):** reiterates **Outperform, TP $315**; calls AMZN the leading infra player, expected to lead hyperscalers in getting compute online thanks to BOM-cost advantages amid tightening/rising-BOM supply. **Trainium ~$225B revenue commitments**; Trainium2 ~30% better price/perf vs comparable GPUs and largely sold out; Trainium3 (shipping early-2026) 30-40% better price/perf than Trn2, nearly fully subscribed; Trainium4 (~18mo out) already largely reserved. **Graviton** advantage in sharper focus amid constrained CPU supply (up to 40% better price/perf vs x86; Meta signed for "tens of millions of Graviton cores"). Chips >$20B ARR, +40% q/q. Op margins seen healthy in **low-to-mid 30%** range; AWS OI $14.2B / ~38% margin (+270bps q/q), with custom-ASIC efficiency a margin offset. Data-gravity (enterprises running AI where data resides) cited as a structural AI-revenue driver (Bernstein "Cloud in the Quarter", 2026-06-22).
- **SemiAnalysis "Anthropic Growth & Bedrock Mix" (2026-05-27) — the Bedrock margin engine, quantified:** the clearest third-party teardown of *why* AWS margins inflected. AWS EBIT margins rose **+213bp Q/Q** — while Oracle, CoreWeave and Azure saw flat-to-declining cloud margins and GCP's rise is "illusory" (see GOOG below) — driven primarily by customer spending growth on **Claude through Bedrock**. The structural edge is the deal mechanic: on Bedrock, **Anthropic is seller-of-record and books the full gross token revenue, while AWS collects BOTH an EC2-like infrastructure fee AND a distribution/rev-share fee** — the latter is what makes TaaS structurally higher-margin than wholesale AI IaaS. Bedrock is a **~$5.5B run-rate business** with **80-90%+ of customers on Anthropic models**, now **37% of AWS AI revenue (up from 9% in 1Q25)** vs AI IaaS still 80%+ of the mix at Azure/GCP; it grew **170% Q/Q in 1Q26** (60% in 4Q25). Punchline: **Bedrock drove ~30% of the Y/Y AWS gross-profit-dollar step-up despite being only ~4% of total AWS revenue**, and SemiAnalysis models Bedrock rising to ~53% of AWS AI revenue in 2Q (adding ~9 points to total AWS revenue growth), with Anthropic Bedrock revenue ~$26M/MW in 1Q26 → ~$42M/MW in 2Q implying Bedrock EBIT margins ~55%. Vertical integration compounds it — Garman: Trainium "now powers >50% of Bedrock token usage"; Graviton4 heads Trn3 nodes; AWS has large CPU/Graviton deals with Anthropic, OpenAI and Meta. AWS also builds more capacity than any CSP in 2025/26/27 and has the **lowest server depreciation (5yr) of all CSPs yet the only one with a true rising-margin trend** (SemiAnalysis "Anthropic Growth & Bedrock Mix", 2026-05-27). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Semianalysis_Bedrock.html)
- **Barclays "AI's S-Curve Is Steepening" (2026-06-01) — AWS margin inflection + capex peak pushed out:** Barclays (Sandler) flags the **AWS operating-margin uptick to 37.7% in 1Q** as evidence of the shift from "bare metal" to **inference-as-a-service** at custom-silicon hyperscalers driving infra-layer margins higher (token efficiency up + revenue-per-token up at the labs). Frames the AI S-curve as steepening on Claude Code/Codex adoption; the **capex peak now pushed beyond 2028 with ~36 GW of next-gen compute** (vs a prior ~2028 peak), and Barclays' hyperscaler-capex aggregate (incl. AWS) goes to **$674B (2026E) → $919B (2027E) → ~$1.16T (2028E)**. AWS is a primary inference hyperscaler for the labs — modeled to challenge Azure for the OpenAI lead by '28 — with AWS contracts of **$38B (2025-32) + $100B (2026-33) for OpenAI** in Barclays' schedule. Reiterates **OW/Positive, PT $330** (Barclays, 2026-06-01). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Barclays_U_S_Internet_Semiconductors_AI_s_S_Curve_Is_Steepening.html)
- **Bernstein Gen-AI Handbook + BofA "AI 2030" — TAM/framework context (historical):** Bernstein's 247-page Handbook (2026-06-22) groups AWS among the four hyperscalers (MSFT/AMZN/GOOG/ORCL) selling IaaS-PaaS and argues that AI accelerates cloud migration of critical workloads through 2030, growing the IaaS-PaaS TAM — Amazon a named beneficiary of incremental database/compute usage (Bernstein "Scaling Intelligence", 2026-06-22, thematic). BofA's older "AI 2030" note (Vivek Arya, 2026-05-13) sizes the AI-accelerator TAM at **~$1.2Tn by CY30** on increased hyperscaler custom-ASIC shipments, naming **AWS Trainium** alongside Google TPU as the diversification driver — historical TAM color consistent with, but superseded in granularity by, the SemiAnalysis Trainium3 work and JPM's >$225B Trainium-commitment datapoint above.
- **Barron's "Top CEOs of 2026" — Jassy profile (Barron's/Nate Wolf, 2026-06-22):** AWS projected at **$168B revenue in 2026, up 30% y/y**; Amazon logged **$215B of capex in the past two years** following AWS's 2021 Meta cloud deal. Jassy on the April earnings call, cited by Barron's: "We continue to be confident in the long-term capex investments we're making" (Barron's, 2026-06-22). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Barrons_22_June_2026.html)
- **AWS fee hike + acceleration drivers (BofA · Justin Post, 2026-06-30):** AWS **raising fees +20% on select GPU workloads effective July 1**, expected to add **1-2pt to 2H26 AWS growth**; acceleration further driven by **OpenAI models added to Bedrock**, capacity ramp for the **$38bn OpenAI commitment**, and early ramp of the **$100bn Anthropic commitment** (BofA · Justin Post, 2026-06-30).
- **Internet-capex/capacity/monetization deep-dive — Amazon the "capacity leader," Buy reiterated (BofA "Raising Internet capex ests", 2026-07-07):** BofA (Justin Post) raises sector capex estimates and translates capex into GW capacity and revenue-per-GW. For **AWS (ex-retail): capex $159bn 2026 (unchanged) / $230bn 2027 (vs $196bn prior)**. Amazon **adds the most capacity of the Big-3 — ~15GW over 2026-27 (vs Google +9GW, Meta +~6GW)** — and at the **lowest cost per incremental GW: ~$25bn/GW in 2026 (vs Google $37bn, Meta $45bn)**, reflecting AWS's scale, internal chips (Trainium/Graviton), and a more balanced core-cloud (vs AI) workload mix. **AWS revenue per Cloud GW ~$10.6bn (2026)** (assuming 70% of AWS capacity is Cloud; 15.8GW of the 22.6GW base is Cloud). In the valuation cut, BofA backs out core retail/ads and finds an **implied Street value of ~$59bn per AWS GW** (2027 capacity, 70% for AI/Cloud) — a discount to Google's ~$110bn/GW, which BofA attributes to lower Cloud rev/GW yield and less perceived AI/LLM differentiation. Recent Anthropic & Google–SpaceX capacity deals at **>$40bn/GW (up to ~$50bn/GW for specialized AI capacity)** — well above the $10-17bn/GW BofA models for AWS/Google Cloud — signal upside to Cloud-revenue estimates. **Reiterate Buy, PO $310.** Biggest 2Q concern: higher capex / lower FCF overshadowing strong revenue (BofA "Raising Internet capex ests", 2026-07-07). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bofa_on_Capex.html)
- **Trainium3 unit demand estimates raised (UBS · Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01):** UBS lifts Trainium3 unit demand estimates to **1.8mn units (2026E) / 2.8mn units (2027E)**, up from a prior **1.7mn/2.3mn** (UBS · Sunny Lin, 2026-07-01).
- **GS Q2'26 earnings preview — Buy, PT raised $325→$335; the four key debates framed (GS "Amazon Q2'26 Earnings Preview", Eric Sheridan, 2026-07-07):** GS reiterates **Buy and raises its 12-mo PT to $335 (from $325)**, price $245.98 (~36% upside), and shifts to a **SOTP methodology** (16x EV/GAAP-EBIT NA + 1.3x EV/Sales Int'l + **29x EV/GAAP-EBIT AWS, up from 28.5x**). GS raises FY26-28 estimates: **revenue $827.6B/$952.9B/$1,081.0B** (FY27 up from $946.6B); FY27/28 EPS trimmed slightly to $10.15/$12.84 on higher D&A. The note frames the intra-quarter stock volatility (downward trend) around four debates: **(1) Consumer health** — GS reads Prime Day (sales +9.5% YoY to ~$26.4B per Adobe; ~42% of orders <$20, ~$23 avg item; Numerator) as a **stable-but-price-sensitive consumer** trading down into staples/essentials, with purchasing strength concentrated in higher-income households. **(2) AI capex & ROIC** — GS **raises 2027-2030 AWS capex** on demand/supply imbalance + input-cost inflation (DC construction, 3rd-party chips, memory), now modeling **~$827B cumulative capex 2026-28** and **AWS ~$200-250B of 2027 capex** (cost to build 1 GW = **$50-60B**: ~$15B physical + $35-45B IT hardware); capex-to-revenue lag 0.5-2.0 yrs; FCFE goes negative in 2027 (~$(41)B) for the first time — funded via debt (avg cost of debt 3.17%→4.45%), no equity needed. **(3) AWS growth** — GS raises AWS revenue: **~33% YoY 2026, ~35% 2027, ~29% 4-yr CAGR '25-'29**; +32% Q2'26E; backlog-conversion framework implies ~10-20% upside to current AWS numbers; a potential **20% EC2 price hike in 2H26 = ~$2-4B net rev tailwind** (EC2 ~50%+ of AWS). **(4) Trainium/custom silicon** — GS argues Trainium positions AMZN well and is a **forward operating-margin tailwind**: Trainium2 "largely sold out," Trainium3 "nearly fully subscribed," annual chip run-rate **$20B** (**$50B if sold to 3Ps**), Trainium3 **+40% price/perf vs Trainium2** (which was +30-40% vs comparable GPUs); doesn't expect Trainium to displace NVDA GPUs for training but sees inference-cost focus + supply constraints as supportive. Also: **Rufus rebranded to Alexa+ ($12B annualized incremental sales, assisted 300M customers)**; 451 Research puts AMZN at **~6.85 GW of DC capacity across ~200 facilities (2025), +3.9 GW added in 2025 (doubled 2022 footprint), targeting a doubling of 2025 capacity by 2027**. Key risks: eCommerce/Cloud competition, margin headwinds from investment, regulatory, macro. [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Q226_Earnings_Preview__Addressing_Key_Operating_Themes_Investor_Debates.html)
- **Amazon Kuiper / Anthropic–AWS read-throughs from the JPM SpaceX initiation (JPM "SpaceX — The Next Frontier", 2026-07-07):** JPM's SpaceX note frames **Amazon LEO (Kuiper)** as a D2D/broadband competitor to Starlink — FCC-authorized for **~7,725 satellites but <400 launched to date**, gated by slow non-SpaceX launch cadence (ULA Vulcan / Blue Origin New Glenn) — i.e. Kuiper's ramp is launch-supply-constrained relative to Starlink's 9,600+ constellation. Separately the note's neocloud analysis reiterates **Anthropic renting external compute at a blended ~$29/W** (Hopper + Blackwell) from SpaceX's Colossus — context for the AWS–Anthropic multi-silicon relationship (Anthropic's willingness to pay up across clouds is a compute-scarcity signal). AWS Trainium sits in JPM's AI-DC power model as one of the custom-silicon platforms; see [SPCX.md](SPCX.md) for the full initiation and [ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).
- **2027 IT-capacity build vs peers (Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak et al, 2026-07-01):** MS estimates AMZN adds **~5GW of IT capacity in 2027**, vs META's ~3.5GW and GOOGL's ~9GW (Morgan Stanley · Brian Nowak et al, 2026-07-01).
- **Broadcom-side reads (light) — direct networking sales + Trainium 4 SOIC (JPMorgan · Harlan Sur "Hock Tan @ JPM", 2026-07-07; JPMorgan · Gokul "TSMC 2Q26 preview", 2026-07-08):** two light supplier-side datapoints. (1) On the Hock Tan call, Hock said **Amazon "claims they can do it all themselves" on networking and commoditizes it, so Broadcom sells direct to Amazon** (no white-box/NOS middleman) — and referenced **ByteDance-style HBM consignment as a bad idea he won't do** (HBM integral to the XPU). (2) Gokul's TSMC preview flags **AWS Trainium 4 moving to TSMC SOIC**, with the merchant Vera/Venice CPUs' CoWoS **heavily dependent on OSATs (ASE/Amkor)** — a packaging-roadmap read on the Trainium 4 (Alchip-backend) ramp already on the page. (JPMorgan · Harlan Sur "Hock Tan @ JPM", 2026-07-07; JPMorgan · Gokul "TSMC 2Q26 preview", 2026-07-08)
- **Project Houdini + "SAMDC" — AWS's construction advantage, quantified (SemiAnalysis "The Wild Wild West Of LEGO Datacenters", 2026-07-29):** in a 75-page modular-construction deep dive, **AWS "shows the fastest scaling buildout currently, adding almost 3.9 GW of capacity to the end of 2025,"** and is the reference case for **operator-led modularization** — engineering the spec itself, procuring owner-furnished gear, and handing an integrator only the assembly (a model "effectively confined to the largest hyperscalers" because it means carrying all the cost, inventory and lead-time risk without vendor allocation leverage). The hard numbers:
  - **Project Houdini** re-cuts AWS's standard data-hall map into a **factory-built skid ~45 feet long, transportable on low double-drop trailers engineered to stay under bridges** — squarely a *factory-built white-space* product. It "**cuts deployment from as much as 15 weeks to roughly 2–3 weeks and can eliminate more than 50,000 on-site electrician hours per module**," with skids built in **Houston, Salt Lake City and Topeka**, early deployments in **Texas and South Bend**, and a target of **~25 weeks from construction start to the first server room**.
  - **Governance is the differentiated bit:** instead of relying only on site-level engineering sign-off, Houdini's factory process "**requires separate validation of the design and the physical build**," with **Cupertino Electric** ([PWR](PWR.md)) the main partner responsible for the latter and the design partner on the spec side.
  - **Beyond white space, the shell is changing too:** "**AWS is now rolling out at very large scale their own modular design codenamed 'SAMDC'**" — narrower, more repeatable structures organised around the systems installed inside, giving "**less building per MW, shorter structural spans, and fewer interfaces for field crews to assemble.**"
  - **Why it shows up in the model, not just the press release:** SemiAnalysis's bottom-up on a 50 MW liquid-cooled hall puts full modular at **~36% (7-9 months) faster and ~8% cheaper all-in (~$13.5mn vs ~$14.6mn/MW)**, and values an earlier month to an owner-operator at **~$500k/MW** (GPU depreciation ~$30mn/MW over five years) — ~**$200mn undiscounted, ~$4mn/MW, on an ~8-month lead**. For a hyperscaler carrying its own GPUs, that is the cleanest link from construction method to AWS capex efficiency and time-to-revenue. Governing caveat: "**go-live is the latest of three dates, building ready, power available, and GPUs delivered.**" (SemiAnalysis "The Wild Wild West Of LEGO Datacenters", 2026-07-29)
- **Graviton5 and the AWS custom-CPU strategy (SemiAnalysis "CPUs are Back," 2026-02-09) — HEAVY:** AWS was the first hyperscaler to deploy its own cloud CPUs, via the Annapurna Labs acquisition plus ARM Neoverse CSS reference designs, letting it go direct-to-TSMC/OSAT rather than buying Intel Xeons (p.45). Generational lineage (AWS's own chart, p.46): Graviton1 (2018, 16 cores, 5B transistors) → Graviton2 (2019, 64 cores, 30B) → Graviton3 (2021, 64 cores, 55B) → Graviton4 (2023, 96 cores, 73B) → **Graviton5 (2025, 192 cores, 172B transistors)**. Graviton3 was the first move to ARM Neoverse V1 (2x FP perf vs N1), a 10x7 core mesh (65 cores printed/64 enabled), 4 DDR5+2 PCIe5 I/O chiplets around a central compute die on TSMC N5 with Intel EMIB packaging — "one of the first datacenter CPUs to deploy DDR5 and PCIe5, a full year ahead of AMD and Intel" (p.45-46). Graviton4 moved to Neoverse V2, +50% cores/memory-channels (96 cores, 12-channel), 30-45% speedups, PCIe5 lanes tripled 32→96, added dual-socket support (p.46). **Graviton5** (in preview since December 2025) doubles cores to 192 on Neoverse V3, 172B transistors on TSMC 3nm; L2 stays at 2MB/core but shared L3 jumps from a "paltry" 36MB (Graviton4) to 192MB — extra cache buffers the fact memory bandwidth only rose 57% (12-channel DDR5-8800) despite cores doubling (p.46-47). Architecture evolved to a chiplet design where 2 cores now share one mesh stop (8x12 mesh); AWS didn't disclose die/package configs this round but confirmed "a novel packaging strategy" (p.47). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/CPU_semianalysis_feb_26.html)
- **JPM US Internet 2Q26 Preview — reiterates OW, Dec-26 PT $330, top pick #2 (JPM · Anmuth "US Internet 2Q26 Preview", 2026-07-14):** JPMe **2Q26 net sales $198.6B (guide $194-199B), AWS +32.0% (buyside +34-35%)**; FY26/27/28 revenue **$824.0B / $934.2B / $1,060.6B**, **AWS FY26 +32.4%**, GAAP EPS **$9.00 / $10.38 / $12.95**; op income 2Q26E **$23.9B (guide $20-24B; buyside $24-25B)**, FY26 $105.5B. Capex **$199.6B FY26 (JPMe $204.2B; mgmt ~$200B; buyside $200-210B) → $290.7B FY27**; FCF **–$34.5B FY26 → –$35.7B FY27 → +$1.9B FY28** (JPM shows FCF bottoming 2026-27). Key debates: AWS accel to mid-30s (40% exit?), will AMZN raise the 2026 capex guide + the ROIC of heavy AI spend, Trainium/chip capacity + utilization, consumer/Prime Day. Graviton5 GA; Project Leo capitalization expected 4Q. Investor sentiment "Positive." [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/JPM_JPM_ANMUTH___US_Inte_2026-07-14_5366760.html)
- **JPM 2Q preview call + MS "$1.4T" podcast — the AWS setup into the print (JPM · Anmuth 2Q preview call, 2026-07-14; Morgan Stanley · Nowak "Hyperscale capex to $1.4T", 2026-07-14):** Anmuth reads AMZN as more crowded/loved than Google by a smidge but "the stock doesn't work" despite AWS ~35% — the unlock is **operating-profit beats** (3Q OI guide framed ~$21.5-26.5B); 2026 capex ~$200B (little change, focus is 2027, buyside **$275-400B, most 325-350B**), FCF **burning ~$35B both '26 and '27**, AWS **capacity set to double by end-2027**; Trainium doubt "quieted a lot" (Anthropic adoption, Trn3→Trn4), Graviton underestimated for inference; Prime Day Q3→Q2 shift a modeling wrinkle. Nowak's podcast quantifies the compute: **AMZN ~14 GW (2025) → 36 GW by 2028** (+7 GW this year, +9 next, 2H-weighted), **AWS +35% '26 / +40% '27** on a deliberately conservative **$7-9 incremental revenue per watt (lowest of the group)** → upward bias; **AWS backlog +$110B q/q in 2Q** (to ~$475B, per the companion 07-12 MS note on-page), incremental margins high-30s/40. [JPM call](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_14_jpm_preview.html) · [MS podcast](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_14_brian_nowak_on_hyperscale_capex_raise_to_1_4t_new_.html)
- **Archera expert call — the "capacity wars" tighten AWS's hand; Trainium serves Sonnet but not the biggest models (Archera expert call, 2026-07-13):** an ex-AWS operator's channel view — as of start-Q2, **just to get A100 access on AWS you now need a ≥1-year commitment** (Amazon floated a "1-yr commit to 100 A100s" to a customer who then went to neoclouds), and Amazon is forcing shorter commitments to protect margin. Amazon's late-2025 rule that **any customer with a >$5M annual AWS commitment must go direct** (no partner) is squeezing the 5-7% partner margins. On silicon: **Sonnet appears servable off Trainium2 with "no capacity issues," but Opus 4.28 / Fable show capacity issues — so Amazon is "still all on Nvidia GPUs," making AWS token supply less fungible than Google's TPU-served Vertex.** Structural cost edge on the CPU side: **>50% of AWS's data-center CPUs have been Graviton for two years**, giving Amazon the most BOM headroom of the hyperscalers to absorb the 2026 RAM-price spike (vs Azure Intel/AMD-only, Google's under-deployed Axion). Bedrock cited as a US-vendor middleware wrapper enabling enterprise adoption of open-source (incl. Chinese) models. **Net-new from the raw Jefferies transcript ("Aran K", 2026-07-13):** unlike Google/Azure — which are "fairly forward-footed" on leasing third-party capacity to fill the demand gap for their customers — **Amazon is "less prone" to lease external capacity** (for competitive-advantage reasons); and both AWS and Google are **prioritizing inference capacity over accelerating Rubin deployment** (both see inference as the bigger near-term opportunity, vs neoclouds racing to deploy Rubin first). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_07_13_archera_13_jul_26.html)
- **Archera/JEF cloud call — CSP terms tightening in the clouds' favor (@SouthernValue95, 2026-07-14):** a second read of the Jefferies-hosted Archera call corroborating the "capacity wars" bullet above — customers trying to renew 3-yr deals are being told they **can only renew 1-yr (smaller discounts = higher price)**; reserving 3-yr capacity now requires **bigger minimum commitments (rising min cluster size) + annual spend cadence**; **GPU and CPU prices going up**; supply > demand, so when clients push back on terms the clouds are **"pushing them to $ORCL or the neoclouds"** (neocloud momentum framed as demand overflow, not share loss). Read as positive for the AI/DC trade incl. AMZN/GOOG, with under-appreciated ROIC upside on existing CSP DC fleets (@SouthernValue95, 2026-07-14).
- **Bedrock multi-model breadth — GPT-5.6 now GA (OpenAI/@gdb, 2026-07-14):** OpenAI's Greg Brockman confirms **GPT-5.6 is now generally available on AWS Bedrock** — incremental proof of the multi-model Bedrock platform thesis (Anthropic + OpenAI + open-source under one AWS-hosted roof), the distribution-endpoint dynamic the sell-side keeps flagging (OpenAI/@gdb, 2026-07-14).
- **AWS network topology "RNG" (Resilient Network Graphs) (@SemiAnalysis_, 2026-07-14):** SemiAnalysis details Amazon's latest DC network topology **unveiled in May — "RNG" (Resilient Network Graphs), built on a random-graph concept rather than the traditional fat-tree** used across the industry — a networking-architecture differentiator underpinning AWS's large-cluster scale-out (relevant to the Trainium/Project Rainier build). (@SemiAnalysis_, 2026-07-14)
- **AWS CoWoS allocation: share slips, wafers still +54% — read the denominator, not the percentage (Fubon "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates", Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang, 2026-07-28):** in Fubon's TSMC CoWoS customer-mix forecast, **AWS runs 14.1% (2023) → 12.4% (2024) → 12.7% (2025) → 10.9% (2026) → 10.4% (2027)** — but the wafer counts behind those shares are **18,200 → 37,220 → 85,650 → 127,500 → 196,500**, i.e. **+49% in 2026 and +54% in 2027**, on a total pool going **675,000 (2025) → 1,155,600 shipped / 1,170,000 capacity (2026) → 1,890,000 (2027)**. **State it plainly: the declining share is a denominator effect, not an allocation cut** — the pool grows +64% in 2027 while AWS grows +54%, so Trainium wafers keep compounding hard even as NVDA holds 50.0% (945,000 wafers), AVGO takes 22.3% (421,800) and MediaTek jumps 1.5% → 7.3% (17,400 → 137,700) on the Google TPU program. AWS remains the **third-largest CoWoS customer** in the book behind NVDA and AVGO, and ahead of AMD (7.0%, 132,300) and MSFT (1.0%, 18,900 — Microsoft entering the mix for the first time in 2026). Useful as an independent supply-side cross-check on the Trainium3/Trainium4 volume marks already on this page (UBS · Sunny Lin: 1.8mn units '26E / 2.8mn '27E) — the wafer trajectory is directionally consistent with that unit ramp. (Fubon, 2026-07-28) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html)
- **Anthropic-Samsung foundry read-through (@jukan05, 2026-07-14):** Korean media reports **Samsung's foundry has secured Anthropic as a customer to manufacture Anthropic's custom AI chips** — a watch item for the AWS-Trainium franchise, since Anthropic (the marquee >$100B Trainium customer / Project Rainier) pursuing its own custom silicon is a potential longer-term diversification away from single-sourcing on Trainium. Unverified single-source report; flagged as a read-through, not a confirmed shift (@jukan05, 2026-07-14).

**Q2'26 guide (Q1 call):** net sales $194–199B; OI $20–24B. Headwinds flagged: ~$1B y/y LEO cost step-up and higher transport/fuel costs (partly offset by FBA surcharge); memory/component costs "skyrocketed." The Q2 top line is helped by **Prime Day moving into 2Q for the first time since 2021** — the 4-day event (June 23–26, 23 countries) shifts an estimated **~$7B–$8B of incremental global revenue into 2Q26, already reflected in the $194–199B guide** (JPM est. ~6% y/y growth/day in 3P and ~7% in 1P, excluding ad/sub upside) (JPM/Anmuth — Prime deep-dive, 2026-06-23). Bernstein (Mark Shmulik, 2026-04-30) calls the OI guide "very conservative" and reads the rev guide as implying continued AWS acceleration into "low-to-mid 30s." Jassy framed AI demand as **"barbelled"** (Q4'25 call) — AI labs + runaway apps on one end, enterprise productivity/cost-avoidance on the other — with the durable middle (enterprise production workloads + net-new AI-native apps) still to come.

## Debate / thesis
- **Post-Q2'26: the ROI debate got an answer, the FCF debate got worse (2026-07-30).** The two halves of the print pull in opposite directions and both are now on the page permanently. **For the bulls:** AWS +36.7% is the fastest in 18 quarters and *accelerating* off a $150B base, the backlog went $364B → **$496B** in one quarter, AWS margin *expanded* to 39.4% while capex ripped, and management put hard numbers on payback for the first time — **<3 years to break even on servers/networking, ≥5-6 year useful life, ≥5-year contracted terms on most AI capacity, and 5-6 server generations off a single 30+ year shell**. That is the specific rebuttal to the "circular / unprovable ROI" bear case that drove the July de-rating, and it is why the AI-supply-chain complex (ALAB/MRVL/CRDO) traded up on someone else's print (MS Wigg, 2026-07-30). **For the bears:** **FCF was −$9B against consensus −$1B**, and FY26 capex went **~$200B → ~$220B with the increment attributed to memory-cost inflation** — Vital Knowledge's framing is the cleanest: "the only two numbers that anyone will talk about are 37% and 39.4% … but people should also talk about −$9B, as this speaks to the cash pressures facing hyperscalers" (Vital Knowledge, 2026-07-30). Note what that $20B capex increment actually is: **not more compute, just a higher price for the same compute.** It is the same memory squeeze that took AAPL's underlying gross margin to ~46.5% the same evening, arriving on the hyperscaler side as capex dollars rather than COGS. **Our read:** the ROI disclosure is the more durable of the two facts, because it is structural (asset-life arithmetic) while the FCF drag is timing (spend precedes monetization by ~2 years, by management's own description). The thing to underwrite is not the FCF print but whether the ~40% AWS exit rate the buy side now models (UBS Dwyer, 2026-07-30) is supply-constrained — management said demand exceeds supply through 2027, which caps upside as much as it de-risks it.
- **Contrast with MSFT/META the night before is the real signal (2026-07-30).** Three-for-three on cloud beats (MS Wigg): MSFT Azure +43% → guided +45%, GOOGL Cloud +82%, AWS +36.7%. But the *capex* messages diverged sharply — MSFT held CY26 capex at ~$190B (the headline drop to ~$175B was a lease-classification change, not a spend cut), META narrowed to $130-145B and raised only the low end, while **AMZN raised outright to ~$220B**. The market rewarded MSFT (+9-15%) and AMZN (+8-10%) and punished META (-8-10%), which tells you the discriminator is not the level of spend but whether accelerating cloud revenue is visibly arriving alongside it. AMZN and MSFT have that; META is still selling a promise (JEF Wong "Reading the Tech Leaves", 2026-07-30; Bernstein Seidman, 2026-07-30).
- **Bull:** AWS re-acceleration is the core long. AI demand "exploding," dragging core compute with it (post-training, RL, agentic tool-use) — Jassy, Q1'26. Custom-silicon edge is the differentiator: Trainium 30–40% better price/perf vs comparable GPUs, lets AMZN "save billions on capex and extract points of margin leverage" (Shmulik, Bernstein 2026-04-30) — the only hyperscaler to **hold capex flat** while peers raised on input-cost inflation, signaling supply-chain discipline. Backlog breadth improving: ~$20B of the sequential step-up is **non-frontier-lab**, the highest on record (Shmulik). Jefferies named AMZN a **top 2H26 pick** (Brent Thill, briefing 2026-06-06); at the Jefferies internet conf (2026-06-01) AWS/Bedrock cited as the structural winner as enterprises push net-new app builds to the cloud. MS (Brian Nowak) flags SpaceX $50/watt compute deal as ~16% upside to AWS '27 rev if monetization reaches $25/watt (briefing 2026-06-18). Into 1Q'26, JPM (Anmuth) framed AMZN as the "AI laggard→winner" transition with the OpenAI deal adding ~$100B to backlog at the print and AI ARR >$15B / chips >$20B (JPM internet preview, 2026-04-15); BofA (Fenske spec-sales) called AWS "my favorite business," with Jassy's redemption arc (OpenAI $50B + Anthropic = "two AI winners solidified," Trainium3/4 sold out, ~$50B if Trainium were an external chip program vs Broadcom's ~$100B AI-semi goal) (BofA, 2026-04-16). Fresh buyside corroboration of the reaccel long: BofA's informal buyside bogey poll now has AWS **reaccelerating from +34.3% Q2 to +35.7% Q3** (reported growth) — slightly above GS's ~33%/~35% '26/'27 — i.e. the AWS-reacceleration long is now the buyside base case, not just a sell-side call (BofA · Brian Fenske (buyside bogey poll), 2026-07-12). [JPM](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_15_jpm_internet_preview_15_apr_26.html) · [BofA](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_16_fenske_16_apr_26.html)
- **AWS margin durability + 1Q'26 print read-throughs (multiple recaps, 2026-04-30):** post-print callbacks corroborated the AWS-acceleration long. AWS grew 28% with **1Q AWS margins better than expected and "no one-timers"** (mgmt reframed the historical "high-20s to low-30s" range as effectively just "low-30s" — no longer expecting the low end); Trainium saves AWS **"several hundred bps" of margin vs renting Nvidia GPUs** (first hard quantification); **Bedrock model-access revenue is booked NET (not gross)** — margin/quality positive (expert recap, 2026-04-30). Citi adds AWS margins are lower now only because it's a "newer-stage, more capital-intensive" business and should recover toward prior levels at scale (Citi C1Q26 recap, 2026-04-30). On capex, AMZN said supply-chain/memory inflation was anticipated and FY26 $200B unchanged, but the expert reads a **~+20%/unit cost impact landing in 2027 (~$235B vs $200B same-units)** (expert recap, 2026-04-30). BofA preview math: ~$150B of the $200B FY26 capex is AWS-driven (~100% of AWS revenue), implying a build toward ~$400B AWS revenue; AI-deal margins are lower (~10–20%, hard to pin) and a near-term Q2 margin risk as Anthropic ramps (BofA/Post preview, 2026-04-20). [Sources](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_30_hyper_wrc_recap_30_apr_2026.html) · [Citi recap](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_30_msft_wfc_c1q26_recap_30_apr_26.html) · [BofA preview](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_04_20_fenske_20_apr_26.html)
- **AWS as a frontier-model distribution endpoint (UBS Karl Keirstead, 2026-06-17):** UBS's "hyperscaler bull case" argues the AWS/GCP growth accelerations can't be explained by per-hour accelerator rental alone — the hyperscalers are increasingly the **distribution endpoint** for the frontier labs (enterprises rolling out Claude Code report "no commercial relationship with Anthropic — we procure it through AWS/Azure"). Lower gross margin, but a powerful upsell and a structural growth accelerant; UBS is an advocate but flags investor mood on AMZN/MSFT/ORCL "more mixed than I'd have thought" despite the revenue beats (UBS "tokenmaxxing", 2026-06-17). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_06_18_karl_ubs_18_jun_26.html)
- **Trainium economics / the silicon long (SemiAnalysis "AWS Trainium3 Deep Dive," 2025-12-04):** Most detailed third-party validation of the custom-silicon thesis. Trainium3 (GA + Trainium4 announced at re:Invent) is a "step-function" upgrade — N3P node (from N5), 144GB HBM3E 12-hi (Hynix/Micron, up from sub-par Samsung on Trn2), +70% memory bandwidth, doubled MXFP8 FLOPs + new MXFP4 support, ~1,000W TDP. New all-to-all **switched** scale-up fabric: NL32x2 air-cooled ("Teton3 PDS") is the volume SKU in '26; NL72x2 liquid ("Teton3 MAX") is AWS's answer to Nvidia's GB200 NVL72 — AWS is the **first non-Nvidia vendor to ship a switched scale-up rack** (ahead of AMD MI450X). Core economics: Trainium gives up marketed FP8/FP4 density vs Nvidia/AMD, but systems are "dramatically cheaper" because AWS **avoids the margin stacking embedded in Nvidia servers** → materially lower upfront cluster capex per logical chip; lower operating TCO driven mostly by chip TDP (Trn2 ~500W, Trn3 ~1,000W vs ~1,200W GB200 / 1,400W GB300). Caveat: no native FP4 support → higher TCO per marketed FP4 FLOP than Nvidia. AWS is open-sourcing its software stack (PyTorch backend, NKI compiler, then XLA/JAX) to build a CUDA-style ecosystem; LNC=8 (the mode mainstream researchers want) not supported until mid-2026. AWS's air-cooling-first datacenter design (vs peers' DLC pivots) is a time-to-market + fungibility advantage, benefiting Project Rainier ramp. Bottom line: a credible NVDA challenger on perf-per-TCO, alongside Google TPUv7 and AMD MI450X — "Nvidia stays King as long as Jensen keeps accelerating." Supply-chain read-through: Marvell **lost the Trainium3 socket to Alchip** (poor Trn2 execution); Alchip leads Trn4 backend (UALink + NVLink-Fusion tracks). UBS (2026 overview) sees MRVL retaining only "some revenue for Trainium 3" via the Celestial AI acquisition — i.e. MRVL is no longer the lead designer.
- **SCORECARD / BACKFILL — the Sept-2025 "AWS AI Resurgence" call and its 2027 tail risk (SemiAnalysis "Amazon's AI Resurgence: AWS & Anthropic's Multi-Gigawatt Trainium Expansion", Ontiveros/Patel/Kourabi/Xie, 2025-09-03 — ingested 2026-07-24, ~11 months old; HISTORICAL structural context, supersedes no 2026 figure on this page):** worth keeping on file as a **scorecard document**. At the exact moment the market was marking Amazon as the YTD laggard among the four AI titans, SemiAnalysis made an explicit out-of-consensus call — an **"AWS AI Resurgence," forecasting acceleration "beyond 20% year-over-year growth by the end of 2025"** — against the consensus "cloud crisis" framing it had itself authored in March-2023. Frame: **AWS ≈ 60% of Amazon group profits**, dominant in classic cloud but losing the GPU/XPU-cloud era to Azure (on OpenAI) and a narrowing Google gap (on TPU). **How the call scored:** the management-commentary table below shows AWS +17.5% (Q2'25) → **+20.2% (Q3'25)** → +24% (Q4'25) → +28% (Q1'26) — the >20% threshold was cleared on the Q3'25 print and the acceleration ran well past it, so the directional call played out on this page's own numbers. The physical forecast also landed: SemiAnalysis counted **>1.3GW of IT capacity across three AWS campuses in final construction dedicated solely to Anthropic training, with just under 1 million Trainium2 in the largest campus** ("AWS is building datacenters faster than it ever has in its entire history"), which reads directly onto JPM's later confirmation of **>1M Trainium2 (Project Rainier)** and JPM's 07-07 sizing of New Carlisle at **~910MW**. At the time SemiAnalysis judged those datacenters were generating **no meaningful revenue yet** (Trainium assembly-phase yield issues). **Diagnosis of the GenAI underperformance (still the live structural weakness):** the custom **EFA** backend fabric lags InfiniBand, Spectrum-X and RoCEv2 (Cisco/Arista/Juniper) on both raw performance *and* user experience (EFAv4 improving but still behind), and AWS's custom networking slows its time-to-market on Nvidia systems. **Trainium2 vs GB200, as of Sep-2025:** GB200 had a **3.85x FP16 FLOPs advantage (2,500 vs 667 TFLOP/s/chip)** and **2.75x memory bandwidth (8,000 vs 2,900 GB/s)**; scale-up aggregate bandwidth **576 TB/s (NVL72) vs 186 TB/s (Teton2-PD-Ultra-3L) = 3.1x** — but Trainium2 was **highly competitive on TCO per million tokens and TCO per TB/s of memory bandwidth**, precisely the axis Anthropic's memory-bandwidth-bound (rather than FLOPs-bound) RL roadmap runs on. Roadmap called at the time: Teton PD / Teton PD Ultra in the field → **Teton PDS and Teton Max in volume in 2026**, introducing an all-to-all scale-up network, **NeuronLinkv3** (4 switch trays mid-rack, 16 compute trays above and below) — i.e. Trainium's architecture explicitly converging toward NVLink/NVL72 (the direction the 2025-12-04 Trainium3 deep-dive above then confirmed). **The 2027 tail risk — the real alpha in the note, and still unresolved:** Trainium shipments match SemiAnalysis's forecast Anthropic IT capacity in 2025–26, but **there is a gap in 2027** — "Anthropic will be essentially the sole customer for Trainium2+ in 2025 and 2026 but isn't large enough to absorb the volumes AWS currently aims to produce in 2027," with **no sizeable external Trainium customer found** (the Trainium1/Inferentia2 precedent), so the risk is "a more visible **degradation of AWS' ROIC profile**." Mark it against what has since partly answered it on this page — OpenAI's **~$100B+ / ~2 GW Trainium deal (Feb-26) starting 2027**, >$225B of total Trainium commitments, Jassy's "good chance" of selling Trainium racks externally — but the ROIC question itself is the same one GS/JPM/Redburn are still litigating in 2026. **Bedrock, as of Sep-2025 (an 11-month turnaround, not a current criticism):** rate limits were "a huge blocker for production deployments" — new accounts got **2 RPM for Claude Opus against an advertised 50**, vs Anthropic direct at 50 RPM and **4,000 RPM at Tier 4** — such that "deploying Claude Code using the Bedrock API can cause Claude Code to crash"; and the **GPT-OSS deployment was botched** (the system prompt the model saw was mutated in "random and incoherent ways"), scoring **13% lower than most other inference endpoints** per Artificial Analysis, with SemiAnalysis concluding "the Bedrock team is still struggling to independently deploying a model at full capability." Read against the 2026-05-27 SemiAnalysis note above — which credits Bedrock with driving the entire AWS margin inflection — this is the single sharpest measure of how far the platform moved in under a year. **Internal models:** Nova and Titan "perform poorly" across the board and have seen "little to no updates" since release; Amazon integrates **Claude, not Nova**, into its own products (Alexa+), and "Amazon does not possess the required talent to produce a strong model" — the structural reason the *Nova-as-a-hedge* idea floated in the 06-30 R&Co repricing story is a weak hedge. Supply-chain read-through on the design partners (Marvell/Alchip/Broadcom, HBM procured direct) is carried on [MRVL.md](MRVL.md) and [custom-asic-tpu](themes/custom-asic-tpu.md).
