**Síntese do quarter:** _(new window - Q2'26 opened 2026-07-30)_ The print resolved the single biggest bear objection of the July de-rating — that hyperscaler AI ROI was unprovable — with a specific quantified payback framework (<3yrs to server break-even, 5-6 server generations per 30-yr shell) rather than qualitative reassurance, and it did so on an accelerating top line (AWS +36.7%, fastest in 18 quarters, margin 39.4%). The offsetting negative is cash: **FCF −$9B vs consensus −$1B**, and FY26 capex went ~$200B → ~$220B with the increment attributed to **memory cost inflation** — i.e. the same memory squeeze that hit AAPL's gross margin the same evening is now showing up in hyperscaler capex dollars, not just in COGS. Open question for the window: whether the ~40% AWS exit-rate the bulls now underwrite survives contact with supply. **Updated 2026-07-30 (run-inbox, primary transcript): one material downgrade to the quality of the print, and one upgrade to the option value. Downgrade — ~$1.2bn of the $27.5bn OI is discrete (tariff refunds + an energy-derivative fair-value mark), and management's own clean AWS margin expansion is +520bps y/y, not the +650bps reported; roughly a third of the OI beat vs Street is non-operating, and the AWS margin line is where it sits. That does not break the bull case (AWS still expanded ~520bps clean while capex ripped) but it does mean the "39.4% margin" number quoted across every desk note is flattered. Upgrade — Trainium as merchant silicon moved from sell-side hypothetical to management-stated live conversation, and Amazon confirmed it is building its own frontier model to sit on the Bedrock shelf next to Anthropic's and OpenAI's. Net: the ROI framework still looks like the more durable fact of the print, but the margin proof is softer than day-one framing, and the pricing mechanism Amazon described for recovering memory inflation (new agreements only) is weaker than the tranche-reset mechanism Microsoft's IR described the same day — see the Cost pass-through row above.** **08-03 (run-inbox — Fubon · Shang/Arthur, hosted by Jefferies) adds the first Asia-supply-chain read since the print, and it lands on the two things the print left thinnest. On the silicon: an independent PRODUCTION-side check — total Trainium chip output ~1.6-1.7mn (2026) → ~3mn (2027) — arrives within ~10% of the page's existing UBS Trainium3 DEMAND mark (1.8mn/2.8mn), which is about as good a corroboration as this program gets given AWS discloses no units; both marks stay, on their own bases. On memory cost, which is the weakest link in the print's story (capex raised to ~$220B with the increment attributed explicitly to memory, and a pass-through mechanism limited to new agreements): the ODMs who actually procure the parts see NO 2027 memory spec change for AWS by name, are budgeting at full rack prices, and are raising their own capex to fund buy-and-sell of GPUs — i.e. no de-spec escape hatch, but also no sign of demand hesitation at the higher prices. The open item is architectural rather than financial: Trainium 4 (~mid-to-Q3 2027) may disaggregate the networking tray from the compute tray, ODMs say AWS has not decided, and Hon Hai joins as a new AWS rack supplier — worth tracking because it would change the switch/optics content of an AWS rack, and because the same ODM is already stretched on Google's TPU rack assembly. **08-05 (FUNDA · Deep\|LLM) adds no numbers to the quarter and is not meant to — it supplies the frame the ROI debate has been missing, and it is the frame we should be arguing inside. FUNDA's claim is that the market's application-lens calculation (find the next coding-like use case, check whether revenue covers capex) is the wrong one, and that the right one reads capex as a DUAL investment: real current returns from AI-driven cloud growth PLUS an option on having enough compute when the recursive-self-improvement threshold arrives. AMZN is the cleanest current-return leg of that argument (AWS +37%, fifth straight quarter of acceleration, and Jassy saying ~$220B "still isn't enough"), and AWS Bedrock is named as one of the three paid-conversion layers in the token → cloud-revenue → capex loop. The discipline it imposes is the risk tiering, and it is the part to hold: strategic necessity is NOT shareholder return — scenario A (RSI threshold ~2027, FUNDA's base case) validates the current tape; scenario B (2029+) is a capex DIGESTION period in which utilisation, depreciation and generational obsolescence do the damage and levered weak-cash-flow players are forced out; scenario C (continual learning unsolved) puts spend beyond genuine inference demand at risk of impairment. Note what that does to the page's existing spread of views: it is the first framework here that makes the AMZN bull case (Jassy's <3yr payback, MS's 25-50% ROIC) and the Redburn $230 bear case functions of the SAME variable — RSI/adoption TIMING — rather than of disagreements about margin disclosure. On this reading the thing to monitor is not the next quarter's AWS growth rate but whether the capability line keeps compressing (FUNDA's own indicators: monthly model iteration, one month pre-training-to-release, three weeks to a price cut), because that is what decides which scenario AMZN's ~$220B is being spent into.** **RE-EVALUATED 2026-08-12 (/run-inbox, hyperscaler cluster — 7 back-dated sources, 07-22 → 08-12). The quarter's open question ("does the ~40% AWS exit-rate the bulls underwrite survive contact with supply?") now has a two-part answer, and the second half is the one that matters. ① ON LEVEL — the margin is better-quality than the print made it look, because it is a blend of a 40-45% core business, a ~15% Anthropic-training loss-leader, and a Bedrock line whose NET revenue accounting makes it structurally accretive (GS 08-12; corroborated mechanism-by-mechanism by MS 08-04). The recent expansion is a MIX effect — the low-margin training workloads lapping — not a cost miracle, which makes it more repeatable, not less. ② ON DIRECTION — every new source caps it. GS models margins DOWN on depreciation as capex mixes into short-life servers; Nowak says explicitly "I do not think AWS margins are going to get to 50," they will "march into the 40s on a reported basis" and then reinvest. ➜ THE PRACTICAL EDGE: a model carrying ≥50% reported AWS margin in 2028 is now offside two houses that both like the stock, and the reconciliation item is depreciation schedule, not demand. ③ THE PRICING WINDFALL IS REAL AND IT ACCRUES HERE, NOT TO THE CONTRACT PLAYERS — Jefferies (08-10) sizes gigawatt monetisation moving from $8-12bn at the start of the year to $30-50bn now ("3 to 5x in the span of six months"), and the structural point is that hyperscalers hold on-demand/spot/short-contract exposure while [[ORCL]] and [[CRWV]] locked five-year unit economics. ④ TWO HARD FACTS ADDED: AWS contract duration per the 10-K went 5 years → 6 years (vs [[MSFT]] commercial RPO at 2-3 years, mixed with M365); and Trainium partner feedback flipped — "Trainium 1 or 2… not great feedback, but with the NEW ONES coming on we've heard GREAT feedbacks." ⑤ THE ONE GENUINELY NEW BEAR, from the customer side: an Anthropic infrastructure practitioner (07-28) expects a frontier lab to be running ~30% of its capacity on OWN data centres within three years, 60% hyperscalers, 10% neoclouds — the labs are actively engineering their way out of being downstream, and the fleet is fully fungible across GPU/TPU/Trainium/AMD.** **RE-EVALUATED 2026-08-13 (2 sources: Morgan Stanley · Nowak 08-12, UncoverAlpha 08-13). The quarter's newest debate — whether cheap open-weight models deflate the token line the whole AWS AI thesis rests on — got its first direct answer, and it lands in Amazon's favour: MS's argument is that compute is the scarce resource, that lower per-token pricing arrives with substantially more volume, and that "EBIT DOLLAR GROWTH MATTERS MORE THAN PERCENTAGE MARGINS HERE," with API token revenue on open models explicitly framed as a possible loss leader — "like milk in a grocery store" — that pulls storage, database, security and rental attach behind it. MS keeps AMZN OW on lowest-cost-to-serve and names custom silicon plus request batching (deepest enterprise footprint ⇒ highest volume of requests to batch) as the two throughput levers only a hyperscaler owns. That is the same conclusion GS reached from the margin side on 08-12, reached independently. ⚠️ The offsetting item is the sharpest one on the page against the silicon story, and it comes from outside the sell side: UncoverAlpha (08-13) argues Trainium's cost-per-token competitiveness "is due to Amazon PUSHING FOR VERY LOW MARGINS," not only to the chip — which is consistent with GS's ~15% training-book EBITDA and which quietly discounts the merchant-Trainium optionality the Q2 call opened, since a merchant price has to carry a vendor margin the captive price does not. ➜ NET FOR THE WINDOW: the open-weight bear is answered on the cloud layer and left standing on the model layer, and the thing to audit next is ATTACH (storage/database/Graviton pull-through), which AWS does not disclose. On the marks: MS's base PT is unchanged at $335 but BOTH scenario legs moved up ($400→$410 bull, $210→$215 bear) and the '27 AWS growth estimate went 40.2% → 40.8%, the page-high.** **🆕 Updated 2026-08-16 (wiki-ingest): two additions, neither of which moves a reported number. (1) MS · Nowak now publishes the SIZED bridge from Jassy's '$1trn AWS' remark to a share price — ~8 incremental GW/yr, monetization rising from ~$8 to $12-15 of REVENUE per incremental watt, 30% LT EBIT margin → ~$500bn company EBIT in 2034-36 → ~$500/share by YE27 (base PT still $335). That is a LONG-DATED frame, not a quarter call: it changes nothing about the '26/'27 print and everything about how the bull case gets underwritten. (2) The supply side of Trainium is now confirmed from the back-end design house (JPM on Alchip), with Trn4 taping out end-2026 — which means the next real test on custom silicon is whether AWS INSOURCES the wafer allocation, not whether the chip lands.** **🔴🔴 08-20 — TWO ASIA CHANNEL CHECKS ON TRAINIUM ON THE SAME DAY, BOTH REVISING UP, AND ~0.5–1.3M UNITS APART ON THE CURRENT YEAR. THAT DIVERGENCE IS NOW THE OPEN ITEM ON THIS PAGE AND IT IS DELIBERATELY LEFT OPEN.** **JPM's Taiwan hardware analyst has the schedule PULLED IN — Amazon *"tried to pull in the schedule one month earlier, SKIP SOME TESTING PROCESS, and just enter mass production in May,"* modules in June — with **full-year T2+T3 at ~2.5–2.8m (1H only ~1.1m)** against an original T3-2H expectation of ~1m, and the direction stated twice (*"we are seeing some upward revision in this Trainium 3"*; *"decent volume uptake recently"*). Its actual call is comparative and is the relative trade against [[GOOG]]/[[AVGO]]: *"compared to TPU, WE FEEL MORE UPWARD REVISION IN TRAINIUM in the Asia supply chain recently."* **BofA's Asia team, same date, has Trainium at 1.5–2m in 2026 → 3–3.5m in 2027 (+~100%).** ⚠️ **NOT AVERAGED AND NOT ADJUDICATED. The likely reconciling variable is which level of the chain and which product generations are counted — JPM explicitly aggregates T2+T3, BofA does not say. For calibration, the page's existing marks (Fubon chip output ~1.6–1.7m 2026 → ~3m 2027, 08-03; UBS unit demand 1.8m/2.8m) sit on BofA's side for 2026 and below BofA for 2027, so JPM is the outlier high on this year and BofA the outlier high on next. Four marks now coexist; nothing was superseded.** ⚠️ **Weight the JPM leg with its source defect: 13,071 `[silence]` filler lines around ~20KB of real content plus heavy homophone garbling — the numbers are the usable part, the names are not. And "skip some testing process" cuts both ways: urgency and confidence, but qualification margin traded for time on a first-volume generation.** **BofA's second contribution is the MECHANISM, and it is the best external articulation of why AWS's stack is the structurally advantaged expression: hyperscaler capex is still trending up ("not seeing any of the slowdown… despite… news talking about GOOG as well as META might slowing down"), but into 2H26/2027 spend rotates toward ASICs because *"the component cost especially in memory is just getting higher, while they will not be able to fully fulfil their own capex with their free cash flow… they will need to either raise more capital externally, WHICH WILL FORCE THEM TO BE THINKING ABOUT THE MONETIZATION OF AI MODELS instead of continuing to make a huge investment on GPUs for the frontier model training."*** ➜ **Memory inflation plus a funding constraint pushes spend from merchant GPUs to captive silicon and from training to monetisable inference — which is the AWS/Trainium/Bedrock case, and simultaneously an argument that the frontier-training TAM decelerates.** **🔴 And the credit market supplied the one number the equity work cannot: on physically comparable data-centre project bonds, AMAZON-offtake paper prices at 7.9% (Wink TX, 216MW) and 8.8% (Andrews TX, 70MW) against [[CRWV]]-offtake paper at 8.2–14.2% (MS Global Credit, 08-20). Same asset class, up to ~5-6 points cheaper — a market-priced measure of what Amazon's covenant is worth, and the concrete counterweight to the capex/ROIC bear. Amazon is also one of only TWO non-USD issuers this year (with Alphabet), and MS's totals EXCLUDE IG term loans such as the AMZN DDTL, so published issuance understates actual borrowing.** ⚠️ **Guard-rails: the ~$1.4trn 2027 capex figure is an AGGREGATE across six names and NO per-name split is taken from that column-interleaved exhibit; and GS Credit's "$194bn issued as of July 25" (07-27; five names incl. Oracle, ex-SPV data centres) is NOT comparable to MS's $132bn+$25bn split without restating — different perimeters, neither a correction of the other.** **🆕 08-21 (Barclays FICC/Credit "AI Infrastructure Financing — August 2026 Update", 08-20 — one source, credit only, nothing superseded): the quarter's funding sub-plot gets its per-name numbers, and Amazon turns out to be the largest IG borrower in the whole AI complex — $62.0bn USD + $30.4bn foreign = $92.4bn YTD, against GOOG $80.3bn and $25bn each for META/ORCL/SPCX while MSFT issued nothing. Its 3/10 deal at $37.0bn is the single biggest transaction in Barclays' table and it is the only name in the group with 50-year (2076) paper. That is unambiguously an advantage in the debate this page has been having about whether ~$220B of capex is fundable. Two qualifiers keep it from being a clean bull. (a) Amazon paid double-digit new-issue concessions on EVERY tranche of BOTH 2026 deals (+10/+11bp in March widening to +12/+22bp in July), where Alphabet priced February at ~0bp — access is not the same as cheap access, and the concession is the cheapest thing to monitor. (b) Hyperscalers are now 14% of ALL USD IG corporate supply (~17% incl. data-center bonds) and Tech is 37% of IG non-financial supply, "a record for a single sector" if sustained, with Barclays expecting supply to keep GROWING — so Amazon is issuing into the most crowded single-sector technical the IG market has seen.** ⚠️ **And one honest unresolved item, flagged rather than smoothed: Barclays marks the two Amazon-offtake project bonds (216MW BLKPRL and 70MW STNGRY) at 5.98% and 6.42% YTW (+147/+200) on the SAME DATE this page's MS row carries 7.9% and 8.8% for what the megawatts identify as the same two bonds. Neither mark is deleted and neither is averaged; the likely reconciling variable is the metric basis or a bond-label mix-up, and it is written up in the 08-20 Barclays full-log row. The RELATIVE point both sources make is unaffected — Barclays' own table prices CRWV-offtake paper at 7.44-9.87% against both Amazon marks, so Amazon's covenant still buys a large market-priced discount on third-party capacity.** ⚠️ **Guard-rail restated: Barclays' $338bn-YTD aggregate (USD-only, IG+HY+ABS/CMBS+visible HPC hardware, vs $179bn full-year 2025) is a THIRD perimeter alongside MS's $445bn/$217bn and GS's $194bn — three definitions, none a correction of another, restate before comparing.**

_**RE-EVALUATED 2026-08-25 (8 sources: AMAZON IR via BTG 08-19 — a COMPANY PRIMARY, and the most important of the eight; BofA · Justin Post “Project Tetromino” 08-24; SemiAnalysis · Dylan Patel podcast 08-25; MS · Brian Nowak Gen AI ROIC investor-feedback PRIMARY transcript 08-14; MS · Skyler Risom 08-12; plus Tier-2 backfills Citi “Open v. Closed” 06-19 and Damnang / “The Optical Edge” 05-05).** ⚠️ **Duplicate-ingest check run first and it caught two: the Daniel Abraahão expert call (08-14) and the MS GenAI-ROIC pod relay were ALREADY folded by the 08-15 run, and the MS · Charlie Chan Alchip/Trainium4 marks by the 08-16/08-25 runs — none was re-logged; only the Nowak passages the earlier relay dropped were added.** **The quarter’s central claim got the strongest possible corroboration and one uncomfortable qualifier, both from the same source. CORROBORATION: Amazon IR confirms the $220bn is memory-cost-driven at the margin, says “a very very high percentage” of it is contracted, and says the company is still seeing demand into 2028 — which converts the capex line from a bet into an order book, and is precisely what this page said the July de-rating was mispricing.** **QUALIFIER: the payback framework that re-rated the group is a BLENDED number — “nearly three years… across Trainium AND GPUs… across both AI and core services” — so the sub-3-year figure should stop being quoted as an AI-capex payback. AND the memory pass-through has not happened yet: IR dates the protective pre-buys to “mid last year” and says the capex impact is only now “starting” to flow through, which puts the COGS risk ahead rather than behind.** **What is genuinely new and additive is the Bedrock mix disclosure — the MAJORITY of Bedrock tokens already run on Trainium, and Claude 4.6 delivered ~3x the token throughput of 4.5 — because it turns the custom-silicon story from a cost-avoidance argument into a measurable operating-leverage one, and because it sits exactly where MS says the value of custom silicon accrues (the infrastructure layer, not the model layer). Against it, IR concedes usage is shifting toward open-weight models on Bedrock and could not explain why the two most-cited recent Chinese releases are not on the shelf.** **On retail, BofA adds the first dollar-sized picture of the next automation leg (~$530mn by 2029, up to 2.5x throughput per delivery station) against a cost-to-serve base that has already fallen 25% in 4.5 years to $4.33/unit — small money, long duration, and the cleanest reason the retail margin cycle is not finished. Positioning (MS desk, 08-12) says the Street agrees: Amazon is the name the rotation into hyperscalers favours, and it is Jassy’s ROIC framing rather than the AWS growth rate that did it.**_

_**RE-EVALUATED 2026-08-28 (/run-inbox, 3 sources: Barclays · Ross Sandler "A Primer on AI Lab & AI Hyperscaler Unit Economics" 08-28; AlphaSense expert call with a former AWS Bedrock PM 08-27; Jefferies · Volkmann water theme note 08-24).** Two of the three land directly on the **"Bedrock model mix & Trainium attach"** row above, which management left as *"no mix disclosure"* — and the flow now fills part of that gap from outside the company. **✓ CONFIRMA on Bedrock as the model-choice layer, with the ECONOMICS attached for the first time:** the ex-Bedrock PM says enterprises route **"mostly 90/10… to Azure, Vertex, or Bedrock… and NOT to OpenAI and Anthropic"**, and — the genuinely new part — that this is a **price-led** outcome, not a lock-in one: *"AWS doesn't charge any premium. They get discounts from Anthropic… it's actually cheaper to go to Bedrock than to run an API through Anthropic"* because blanket committed-spend cloud discounts apply on Bedrock and not on the lab's own API. He also discloses the strategic history behind the multi-model shelf — Bedrock courted Llama and OpenAI expressly **"to get a second player in so that we can have negotiation power with Anthropic"**, a program he now considers complete (*"with Grok, GPT, and Claude all available, I don't think there is much incentive now"*). ➤ **Net: the Bedrock row moves from "framed as the model-choice layer" to "the model-choice layer is a PROCUREMENT instrument that has already done its work, and its price advantage over the labs is structural to the EDP commitment."** **⚠️ NUANÇA on the pass-through economics, and it is an unresolved conflict rather than a confirmation:** Barclays models **"Indirect Hyperscaler Fees" at 20% of customer spend**, which is hard to square with *"AWS doesn't charge any premium"* — reconcilable if the 20% is a wholesale spread rather than a list markup, but the two are not obviously the same number. **Left open and carried to [`_meta/reconciliation-2026-08-28.md`](_meta/reconciliation-2026-08-28.md).** **On demand visibility:** Barclays sizes the return leg — **$35-40 of every $100 of AI-lab revenue reaches a hyperscaler at ~35-45% OI margin**, with **AWS AI revenue at $3/$10/$37/$87/$151bn ('24-'28E)** — and attaches the one dated bear item of the set: **hyperscalers "likely start to LOSE market share of AI lab training and inference starting in '28 when backstopped AI infrastructure projects come online."** The expert independently reaches ~3 years for the same threat from a stickiness argument, and BBG consensus has [[CRWV]] and [[NBIS]] crossing into profit in the same year — **three unrelated reads converging on 2028, which is the strongest forward-risk corroboration on this page this week.** **A new, small, non-optional cost line also opens:** Jefferies documents **$400m of Amazon water infrastructure alongside the $12bn Louisiana campus**, a Veolia reclaimed-water deal in Mississippi, and a water-positive-by-2030 pledge across all four hyperscalers that **"requir[es] contracted infrastructure rather than just efficiency targets"** — immaterial to earnings, but a **siting** constraint in the fastest-growing DC geographies (Arizona's three-year DC tax-incentive moratorium; litigation in Chile). **Sinal composite: ✓ confirma on Bedrock's strategic position and on AI demand breadth; ⚠ nuança on the indirect-API margin mechanics (open conflict) and on the durability of hyperscaler share past 2027.**_

ONLY THE CUSTOM-SILICON ROW MOVES, AND IT MOVES BY CORROBORATION RATHER THAN BY A NEW NUMBER.**_ The seller of the competing platform independently puts **Trainium at *"30 to 40% better price performance than comparable to other GPU instances"*** on the record — the first non-AWS voice on this page to state that figure — but **scopes it to *"inside within the AWS ecosystem"***, i.e. an ecosystem-bound economic advantage rather than a portable silicon one. ⚠️ **And he attaches the caveat that should now sit on every price/perf mark here, AWS's included: *"there's no credible single public benchmark which… looks across TPUs, Rubins, Trainium 3, MI450, because all these vendors use different precisions, different workloads, system assumptions."*** ➤ **Custom silicon stays ✓ CONFIRMS — the corroboration is real and comes from a hostile witness — but the row now carries an explicit "unbenchmarked by any neutral third party" flag, and a NEW unaddressed risk the call raises for any ASIC: **there is no secondary market for merchant AI ASICs where NVIDIA has a strong one, so the residual-value/depreciation risk sits with the owner.** Nothing on this page has yet sized that for Trainium.

hierarchical fat-tree, using **ShuffleBox passive optical components** and **"Spraypoint" routing** to make flat networks practical at production scale — conventional fat-trees *"require additional MS says no, for FOUR reasons: ① "COMPUTE IS THE SCARCE RESOURCE, and enterprises rely on hyperscalers to provide compute necessary for open-weight inference workloads"; ② hyperscalers monetise to maximise ROIC and lower per-token pricing "may come with SUBSTANTIALLY MORE VOLUME at lower per token or GW unit economics — **EBIT DOLLAR GROWTH MATTERS MORE THAN PERCENTAGE MARGINS HERE**"; ③ both layers stay focused on token throughput and incremental unit economics; ④ all GenAI workloads "drive ATTACH of other services and incremental profitability."** ★ **THE FOUR MONETISATION PATHS — this is the Bedrock thesis argued from the other end: ① serverless API token revenue (Bedrock/Foundry/Vertex), ② accelerator rental for customers self-deploying, ③ RAG — "connecting open models to proprietary data… driving STORAGE AND DATABASE REVENUE," ④ security (governance, guardrails, identity, monitoring). Request batching as a scale advantage has not been on this page before, and it is the one throughput lever a neocloud cannot buy.** ★ **THE IaaS ROIC TABLE, reprinted unchanged — the point being that the band SURVIVES the open-weight price deck (1GW GB300, 410,256 chips, 75% utilisation, $7.6bn/GW opex = $5bn IT dep + $1bn non-IT dep + $2bn energy/other, 21% tax): at $7.00 / $8.50 / $10.00 per GPU-hour → revenue/GW $18.9bn / $22.9bn / $27.0bn, incremental EBIT margins 60% / 67% / 72%, NOPAT $8.9bn / $12.1bn / $15.3bn, **ROIC 23% / 31% / 39%**. 🔴 **REFRESHED AMZN RISK-REWARD — the BULL AND BEAR MOVED, the base did not: OW, **PT $335** (~25X P/E on an average of **$14** of '27/'28 EPS, implying ~28X '27 and ~22X '28); **BULL $410** (~27X avg $15 of bull '27/'28 EPS) — **RAISED from $400**; **BEAR $215** (~17X avg $12) — **RAISED from $210**. The recent margin expansion is explained as the low-margin training mix LAPPING, not as a one-off.** ⚠️ **GS is nonetheless modelling AWS margins DOWN from here, and is explicit that the driver is singular — asked "why are you seeing worse margins going forward, is it just D&A or something else," the answer was "REALLY REALLY D&A. That's really the bigger driver."** Mechanism (quoting Jassy's own 2Q framing back): capex has been going into shells, land, buildings and power (no or very long depreciation) and is now mixing toward **servers, networking and chips at much shorter useful lives** — so the depreciation flow-through "starts to compound this year." GS deliberately **does not underwrite the 50-55%+ incremental margins the buy-side is extrapolating**, and models **non-depreciation AWS expenses growing "low double digits to mid-teens."** ★ **STRUCTURE, useful for modelling: EC2 is ~50% of the CORE (non-AI) AWS revenue line — and core is ~80% of total AWS (GS confirmed the "latter" reading of its own EC2 note when asked directly).** ★ **ON PRICE: the EC2 price-increase thesis is framed as "POTENTIAL UPSIDE DRIVERS… we're not necessarily baking in the benefit." On the GPU/training line specifically GS "would be SURPRISED if they dramatically take margin or raise price on those TRAINING-related workloads" because it would cannibalise the long-dated inference opportunity.** ★ **BEDROCK'S REAL MOAT, in GS's words, is the ROUTING LAYER, not model access: "there's an orchestration and routing layer… that routes the query to the best model from a price-performance balance… if I'm Goldman Sachs, I don't want to put the onus on my employees to maintain all of these different APIs and know which model is best for which query."** And the open-weight mix shift is **NOT** a margin problem — "the benefit of increasing workloads overall… is MORE THAN OFFSETTING that negative mix shift. They might go to a NeoCloud."** ⚠️ **The caveat on those $30-50bn/GW deals: "all those 30 to 50, they have the option for a 90-DAY LEASE CANCEL. So while it could be a two-year contract, three-year contract, you still have the option to cancel in 90 days" — which is why they are not priced like five-year commitments.** ★ **ON AWS MARGIN QUALITY: "AWS margin is NEAR A RECORD HIGH this quarter. I think having the GRAVITON AND THE TRAINIUM helping their cost economics throughout the stack is helping them." Graviton in particular — "a VERY, VERY GOOD CHIP… because it's an ASIC and they don't use any other partners, it actually enables you to [extract] better than you would be able to on the AI front."** ★ **DURATION — a hard 10-K datapoint this page should hold: "a lot of the Amazon backlog is tied to AWS, and per their 10-K their CONTRACT DURATION HAS INCREASED versus last year — it was FIVE YEARS last year and now it's SIX YEARS," versus [[MSFT]]'s commercial RPO which is mixed with M365 at two-to-three-year durations. "AWS definitely has a longer forward-looking RPO."** ★ **TRAINIUM CHECK, and it is a mark-up: "Trainium 1 or 2, I don't think they were great feedback, BUT WITH THE NEW ONES COMING ON, WE'VE HEARD GREAT FEEDBACKS FROM THEM FROM THE PARTNERS THAT WE SPOKE TO."** ★ **BEDROCK VALUE PROP (matching GS's 08-12 read): multi-model access on top of your existing data stack and governance, one vendor relationship, and — the point they stress — "every enterprise has trust[ed] Amazon significantly MORE than they would the other providers. Bedrock USED TO STRUGGLE because you didn't have access to all the models, BUT NOW YOU DO."** Positioning: "**I think Amazon we still love as well.**" [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/2026_08_10_max_jeffries_post_2q26.html) |
| 08-07 | 🔴 **The Information — "AWS TELLS ENGINEERS TO CUT CPU WASTE AMID CRUNCH." An internal-efficiency directive at the exact moment capex is at a record.** | supply / margem | **mixed** | **★ THE ITEM: AWS is instructing engineers to reduce CPU waste in the face of a capacity crunch (The Information, 2026-08-07).** ⚠️ **Headline and framing captured; the internal detail, the scope of the directive and any quantification are NOT in the captured text. Logged as a pointer, not a datapoint.** **WHY IT MATTERS ANYWAY, and why it is filed here rather than dropped: this page carries CY2026 AWS capex around $214bn versus Jassy's ~$220bn guide, on a thesis that spend is demand-constrained. AN INTERNAL DIRECTIVE TO RECLAIM EXISTING CPU CAPACITY IS THE BEHAVIOUR OF A SUPPLY-CONSTRAINED OPERATOR, NOT A DEMAND-CONSTRAINED ONE — it says the marginal cheapest capacity available right now is capacity AWS already owns.** ⚠️ **Two readings and they matter differently: (a) BULLISH — demand exceeds even a record capex plan, so utilisation and pricing hold; (b) BEARISH ON THE CAPEX LINE — efficiency gains reclaimed internally are capacity NOT purchased, which is the mechanism [[NVDA]] bears invoke. This page should not pick one on a headline alone; both are logged and the discriminator is whether AWS capex guidance moves at the next print.** ✅ **Consistent with the same-week evidence from a competitor's disclosure: [[MSFT]] "has been UNABLE TO LEVERAGE THEIR ACCESS TO OPENAI MODELS FOR THEIR API BUSINESS FOUNDRY, OR FOR THEIR APPLICATIONS LIKE COPILOT" for lack of compute (SemiAnalysis, 08-07) — two of the three largest clouds simultaneously rationing internal compute is a supply signal, not a demand one.** (The Information, 2026-08-07) |

Those are all higher-margin transactions."** ⚠️🔴 **AND THE CEILING, stated unprompted and worth holding against the bulls: "I do NOT think that AWS margins are going to get to 50… I've been hearing this the last couple of days — is AWS going to print a 50% margin by 2028? | 08-04 | **Bernstein · Gautam Chhugani — "AI Infra/miners: Texas data center audit to drive more MW scarcity"** | supply | **mixed** | **READ-THROUGH (subject is the ERCOT/Texas data-center audit and the bitcoin-miner / AI-infra cohort, not AMZN) — one AWS-specific counterparty exposure. Per Bernstein, Cipher Mining (CIFR) has 700 MW UNDER CONSTRUCTION for AWS and Fluidstack, part of an approved 1.2 GW that is explicitly UNAFFECTED by the audit** (the rest of that 1.2 GW being **270 MW grid-approved** — 70 MW Texas / 200 MW Ohio — and a **207 MW operational BTC mining site in Odessa, Texas**). ⚠️ **The exposure is INCREMENTAL, not contracted: 2 GW of CIFR's 3.2 GW TOTAL PIPELINE depends on ERCOT "Batch Zero" approval for energization, and ERCOT has PAUSED that review (classification results had been due 2026-08-07) after Governor Abbott directed the PUCT and ERCOT to audit ALL data-center projects in the interconnection queue. So any AWS capacity from this counterparty BEYOND the contracted 700 MW is exposed to the audit delay, while the 700 MW itself is not.** ⚠️ **MW BASES LABELLED AND NOT MIXED: 700 MW = under construction · 1.2 GW = grid-approved · 3.2 GW = total pipeline (2 GW of it Batch-Zero-dependent). None of these is energised IT load — see `_meta/assumptions.md`.** Bernstein's broader conclusion cuts the same way as the capacity-wars bullets already on this page: the audit **"throttles speculative data center pipeline and makes genuine sites with development history more valuable"** → approved MW re-rate, time-to-power lengthens for new builds. Cipher Mining / CIFR is not a wiki-covered name. Cross-ref [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md). Both marks stay on the page.** **On architecture, from Arthur's ODM checks — the generational split is the substance here. Trainium 3: "they are very similar like Nvidia and AMD. Timing: Trainium 4 lands next year, ~mid-to-Q3.** **Net-new and concrete: "AWS will have a NEW SUPPLIER FOR RACK, that will be HON HAI. Read that as ODM capacity being reallocated across the two largest custom-silicon programs, and as a reason to watch whether Hon Hai can serve both.** **Finally, the memory-despec check covers AWS BY NAME: looking at 2027 specs, the ODMs (Hon Hai and "Weichuang" [ASR-garbled Taiwanese ODM]) "don't see any spec change for memory… including the Google, AWS," and their purchasing teams are budgeting at full rack prices while raising their own capex to fund buy-and-sell of GPUs for 2027.** Directly relevant to the **Cost pass-through / Capex** rows above, where memory-cost inflation is the live issue: the buyers of the parts are not planning to de-spec their way out of it. | 08-05 | **FUNDA · "Deep\|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat"** | capital | **mixed** | ⚠️ **Third-party FRAMEWORK, not a mark — no AMZN rating, PT or estimates, and the AMZN figures it cites are the page's own primary-transcript numbers, deliberately not re-added to the body.** **The claim: the market is running an application-lens calculation (where is the next coding-like use case, does current revenue cover capex) while the labs are underwriting RECURSIVE SELF-IMPROVEMENT — so capex should be read as a DUAL INVESTMENT, real current returns from AI-driven cloud growth PLUS an option on having sufficient compute when the RSI threshold arrives.** AMZN is the current-return leg: **AWS +37%, "its fastest in eighteen quarters and its fifth consecutive quarter of acceleration," and Jassy that even after raising 2026 capex to roughly $220B it "still isn't enough to meet all of this year's demand."** **AWS Bedrock is named (with Microsoft Copilot and Vertex AI) as the paid-conversion layer in the loop FUNDA draws — "tokens, paid conversion, cloud growth, reinvestment"** — which is the Bedrock/TaaS thesis on this page arriving from the demand side. ⚠️ **The honest half, and the reason this is logged mixed rather than bull: the group arithmetic does NOT close on current ARR — the four largest cloud providers guided to roughly $725B of 2026 capex, +77% y/y, "absorbing the majority of their operating cash flow," and multi-track commitments (Stargate $500B/9GW; Anthropic–Google up to 1M TPUs) do not pay back on today's revenue. FUNDA answers with option value, then separates it from shareholder return explicitly: "an arms race that is individually rational can compress industry-level returns," with GPU economic life of a few years and token prices falling ~10x/yr as the leakage.** 🔴 **The three-scenario frame, keyed to RSI timing — carry this: (A) threshold ~2027, FUNDA's MOST LIKELY — full utilisation, strategic value realised, value concentrates in the first movers and their compute supply chain; LOW risk. (B) threshold slips to 2029+ — a capex DIGESTION period; utilisation decides who is hurt first, depreciation and generational obsolescence erode returns, levered players with weak cash flow are forced out; MEDIUM risk, "warrants serious weight." (C) longer, or continual learning unsolved — spend beyond genuine inference demand faces IMPAIRMENT; HIGH risk, tail.** **Why it matters for the book: it is the first framework on this page that makes the AMZN bull case (Jassy's <3yr payback; MS's 25-50% GenAI ROIC, 07-27) and the Redburn Street-low $230 bear case functions of the SAME variable — adoption/RSI timing — rather than of a disagreement about margin disclosure. On FUNDA's own scenario-B language the participants forced out first are "highly levered… with weak cash flow," which on this page's funding evidence is the neocloud cohort ([[CRWV]]), not AMZN.** Tape context: **July 2026 was the sharpest drawdown of the cycle — semiconductors' worst month since 2002, SOXX down ~20%, >$1trn of AI-linked market value off** — which FUNDA reads as "a repricing of narrative disagreement rather than an inflection in the capability curve." Cross-ref [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md), [[MSFT]], [[GOOG]]. (FUNDA "Deep\|LLM: RSI Is the Most Important Variable, and Compute Is the Deepest Moat", 2026-08-05) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Funda_on_RSI.html) |

GS's method: start from GIR EQUITY RESEARCH'S CAPEX FORECASTS FOR FIVE HYPERSCALERS — AMAZON, Alphabet, Microsoft, Meta and [[ORCL]] — FY2026 to FY2030, plus actual FY2025 capex; match against actual and forecast CASH FLOW FROM OPERATIONS to assess the trend between sources and uses of cash; then ESTIMATE A DEBT-FINANCED SHARE of hyperscaler capex.** **The number: *"So far in 2026 (as of July 25th) the hyperscalers have issued $194 billion of debt"* — EXCLUDING data-centre financings issued by special-purpose vehicles.** ⚠️ **RECONCILIATION FLAG: GS's $194bn (five names incl. Oracle, ex-SPV data centres, as of July 25) and MS's $132bn "HQ Hyperscalers" + $25bn Oracle YTD through mid-August measure DIFFERENT PERIMETERS. Not comparable without restating; neither is a correction of the other; both are on the page, labelled.** ➜ **The methodological point is the durable part for this page: matching capex against CFO to back out a debt-financed share is exactly the arithmetic behind the "AWS capex now absorbs the majority of operating cash flow" framing already carried here from FUNDA (08-05).** (Goldman Sachs Credit Strategy, 2026-07-27) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/30fd5a85-ed37-468d-bcf4-b0fe152aac3c.html) |

AMZN: $62.0bn USD + $30.4bn foreign = $92.4bn of IG issuance YTD**, vs [[GOOG]] $80.3bn, [[META]] $25bn, [[ORCL]] $25bn, [[SPCX]] $25bn, **[[MSFT]] $0**; group $182bn USD / $65.7bn foreign / **$247.7bn**. Barclays: "AMZN/GOOGL have each issued more IG debt YTD than META/ORCL/SPCX combined," and both "have come to market much more frequently via foreign currency transactions" — **the hard version of the "only Alphabet and Amazon have tapped non-dollar markets" claim this page already carries.** **Amazon's 3/10 USD deal at $37.0bn is the single largest transaction in the table**; then EUR $16,829mn (3/11), CAD $10,093mn (6/8), CHF $3,480mn (5/12), USD $25.0bn (7/7). **Amazon is the ONLY issuer with 2076 paper (a 50-yr $3.0bn 6.05% tranche) — the longest-dated funding in the group.** <br>⚠️ **THE COST SIDE, AND IT IS THE HONEST OFFSET: Amazon paid DOUBLE-DIGIT new-issue concessions on EVERY tranche of BOTH deals — +10/+11bp in March, widening to +12/+22bp in July — where Alphabet's February deal came at −3/+3bp. Secondary held (July paper marked at or inside issue spreads across most of the curve; March paper tightened at the front end, widened modestly past 2046).** ➜ **Amazon has the deepest access and is using it hardest, at a rising concession. Access ✓, price of access ⚠.** <br>🔴 **THE SAME TWO AMAZON-OFFTAKE PROJECT BONDS THIS PAGE CARRIES FROM MS — WITH BARCLAYS' OWN MARKS, AND THEY DO NOT MATCH. Barclays' Figure 7: BLKPRL (Cipher Digital / Black Pearl Compute LLC, 6.125% due 2/15/31, issued 2/4/26, $2,000mn, tenant AMAZON, 216MW critical IT, Ba2/NR/BB-) at price 100.44, YTW 5.98%, OAS +147; and STNGRY (Cipher Digital / Stingray Compute LLC, 6.000% due 6/15/31, issued 6/8/26, $810mn, tenant AMAZON, 70MW, NR/BB+/BB-) at price 98.25, YTW 6.42%, OAS +200.** ⚠️⚠️ **NOT RECONCILED AND NOT ADJUDICATED: the 08-20 MS row above carries 7.9% (216MW) and 8.8% (70MW) for what the megawatts and the Amazon offtake identify as these same two bonds, on the SAME DATE. Barclays' figures are internally consistent (OAS +147/+200 over ~4% Treasuries ≈ its 5.98%/6.42% YTWs) but that is not proof MS is wrong — the reconciling variable is most likely the METRIC (bond YTW vs a project yield-on-cost or an all-in cost-of-capital measure) or a bond-label mix-up in the source PDFs, and possibly a different instrument in the same sponsor programme. BOTH MARKS STAY ON THE PAGE, dated and attributed. What survives the discrepancy unharmed is the RELATIVE conclusion the row was carrying: Barclays' own table prices [[CRWV]]-offtake paper (APLD 7.44%, EDGCOM 9.15%, CORZ 8.35%, ELKGVP 8.64%, ELNFOR 8.12%, GALAXY 9.87%) well WIDE of both Amazon marks, so Amazon's covenant is worth a large, market-priced discount on third-party capacity either way.** <br>🔴 **THE CROWDING CONTEXT, which is net-new: hyperscalers = 14% OF ALL USD IG CORPORATE SUPPLY YTD (~17% including data-center bonds), 21% of IG NON-FINANCIAL supply, and Tech (inclusive of META/AMZN/SPCX) 37% — "which if maintained at that level would be a record for a single sector."** IG hyperscaler supply ~$248bn YTD across currencies, Barclays forecast **~$285bn by year-end**, its investor survey $275-325bn; duration ~32% 1-6y / ~32% 7-15y / ~36% 15y+ incl. ~$88bn 30y-and-out. Barclays expects IG supply to **keep growing**: "the mix is broadening to include equity, but capex is also accelerating." <br>**Aggregate, with the guard-rail attached: ~$338bn of AI/digital-infrastructure financing tracked YTD across IG, HY and ABS/CMBS vs $179bn in FULL-YEAR 2025** (the note's headline rounds to "~$340bn"; the body says $338bn), **+$118bn since Barclays' first tracker on 6/9**. ⚠️⚠️ **DO NOT NET THIS AGAINST THE OTHER TWO AGGREGATES ON THIS PAGE. Barclays' $338bn is USD-only across IG + HY + ABS/CMBS + visible HPC-hardware facilities; MS's $445bn YTD vs $217bn FY2025 (08-20) is "across credit channels"; GS's $194bn as of 7/25 is five names ex-SPV data centres (07-27). THREE PERIMETERS, THREE NUMBERS, NONE A CORRECTION OF ANOTHER — restate before comparing.** <br>**One absence worth logging rather than over-reading: Barclays' visible HPC-hardware facility table (13 facilities, $68.6bn total, $55.2bn in 2026) contains NO Amazon entry — but Barclays explicitly flags "limited visibility to private market transactions," so this is consistent with, and does not confirm, this page's existing MS note that published totals EXCLUDE IG term loans such as the AMZN DDTL.** ⚠️ **Credit desk: no AMZN capex, AWS margin, EPS or rating is derived from any of this.** (Barclays, 2026-08-20) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Barclays_AI_Infrastructure_Financing_Tracking_Issuance_Across_Asset_Classes_August_2026_Update.html) |

| **08-27** | 🔴 **AlphaSense expert call · ex-AWS Bedrock PM (also ex-Google Vertex, ex-Meta PyTorch, ex-Microsoft Azure ML)** — buy-side-led | **demanda / margem** | **bull** | ★★ **The single best account on this page of WHY enterprise AI spend lands on AWS rather than on the labs directly — and it is a pricing argument, not a lock-in argument.** **"Enterprises are, I would say, mostly 90/10 that they will go to Azure, Vertex, or Bedrock to get their API access and not go to OpenAI and Anthropic."** Reasons given: support across geographies, and network stability *"like six or eight nines that Azure has, or AWS has spent years building."* 🔴 **The mechanism is the punchline: "AWS doesn’t charge any premium. They get discounts from Anthropic, and that’s how they’re trying to make money… customers get blanket commitment-based discounts on everything they spend on cloud. And they get that discount if they go to Bedrock; they don’t get it if they go to Anthropic today. So it’s actually CHEAPER to go to Bedrock than to run an API through Anthropic for enterprises."** ➤ **That inverts the usual resale-markup assumption — AWS’s indirect-API margin comes from buying wholesale, not from marking up retail, and the customer’s committed-spend discount makes the hyperscaler the cheap route. It also means Bedrock volume is defended by the EDP commitment, not by switching costs.** ⚠️ **Sits in tension with [[Barclays]]’ same-week framework, which models "Indirect Hyperscaler Fees" at 20% of customer spend — reconcilable if the 20% is the wholesale spread rather than a list markup, but the two are not obviously the same number. Flagged for the reconciliation.** On open-source hosting: hyperscalers pay model providers **10-30% revenue share** (*"15%-20%, 30% is what we have negotiated"*) and still clear **"50% margins, above 50% margins in all of these"** — only about **20% below frontier economics**, and negotiable. Demand: **"inference demand from customers has gone 10X every half-year, and that is underserved today."** ⚠️ **Single expert, buy-side-led call; the expert left AWS in 2024 and speaks to Bedrock-era mechanics. Directional, not disclosure.** (AlphaSense expert call, former Product Manager Technical at AWS (Bedrock), buy-side-led, 2026-08-27). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/--_-_ExpertCall_document_dated_27-08-2026_1.html) |

- 🆕 **2026-08-19 — Nomura (Frank Fan / Donnie Teng), "Unitree Robotics — Initiating coverage on China's embodied-AI leader"** (688836.SS, Buy, TP CNY370): **read-through only, but it is a REGULATORY item on the marketplace rather than a demand item, which is why it is worth a line.** Amazon is named as one of Unitree's online sales channels — *"a hybrid direct-plus-distributor, online- plus-offline go-to-market model, selling through platforms such as Tmall, **Amazon (AMZN US, Not rated)**, and **[[SHOP]]ify (SHOP US, Not rated)**"* — with the **Amazon and Shopify channels together ~10.72% of Unitree's 2025 revenue.** 🔴 **The part that generalises beyond one seller: Nomura flags a rule change under which "online marketplaces must **'VERIFY', not merely display**, authorization status" (cited as **§2.804(c)(2)**), alongside a cut in a per-entry allowance "from 4,000 to 40 units" and a requirement that "certification applicants file **HBOM/SBOM** naming components" (§2.911(d)(9)-(11)). Nomura's own framing: *"directly relevant to the Amazon and Shopify channels, according to the company"*, and *"**Structural, not cyclical**: risks on compliance costs, BOM disclosure, and client adoption."*** ➤ **Read as a compliance-burden vector for the 3P marketplace: shifting the marketplace from *displaying* a seller's authorization to *verifying* it moves liability and process cost onto Amazon, and it applies to a whole category of radio-equipped imported hardware, not just humanoids. Sized at "Medium" impact by Nomura for its own name; NOT sized for AMZN.** ⚠️ **Single-source, from a Chinese A-share initiation, not a regulatory or AMZN-specific note; AMZN is "Not rated" by Nomura here. The rule citations are as printed in the note and have not been verified against the primary regulatory text — do not build on the section numbers without checking them.** Theme: [humanoids-robotics](themes/humanoids-robotics.md). [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Unitree_Robotics_-_Initiating_coverage_on_Chinas_embodied-AI_leader.html)

Three bottom-up GenAI ROIC frameworks on 1GW of GB300 (410,256 chips; $39bn all-in capex/GW = $23bn IT/5yr + $16bn non-IT/15yr at ~$4mn/rack; ~$6bn/yr depreciation; 75% utilisation; $7.6bn/GW opex; 21% tax): hyperscaler IaaS at $8.50/hr → $22.9bn rev/GW, ~67% incremental EBIT margin, ~31% ROIC (23-39% band); model API on owned infra → $30bn/GW, ~75% margin, ~46% ROIC; model API on 3P infra at $7.75/hr → $40.5bn rev less ~$28bn compute, ~31% margin, ~25% NOPAT margin. Explicitly bullish AWS/GCP/Azure, with the caveat that reported near-term incremental margins run below the band. **AMZN risk-reward: OW PT $330 (~25x avg $13 of '27/'28 EPS), bull $400, bear $210; spot $232.11, mean consensus PT $309.55 (range $207-$370), 94% OW; options-implied P(>$330) ~11.3% vs P(<$210) ~39.7%.** Drivers: revenue 716,924 → 826,010 → 957,576 → 1,109,179 ('25-'28e); GAAP EBIT 79,975 → 222,258; **AWS revenue growth 19.7% → 34.9% → 40.2% → 36.2%** (the 40.2% for '27 the page-high mark); MS vs cons FY Dec-26e EPS $9.59 vs $8.82.
  - [Fubon Research (Sherman Shang / Daniel Yang) — "Our advanced packaging, CoWoS forecast adjustments; other updates" (2026-07-28)](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Our_advanced_packaging_CoWoS_forecast_adjustments_other_up.html) — TSMC CoWoS customer-mix forecast: **AWS 14.1% (2023) → 12.7% (2025) → 10.9% (2026) → 10.4% (2027) on wafers 18,200 → 85,650 → 127,500 → 196,500** (+49%/+54%), against a pool of 675k → ~1,156k → 1,890k. Share decline is a denominator effect; AWS stays the #3 CoWoS customer behind NVDA (50.0%) and AVGO (22.3%). Distributed by Jefferies; sector rating Overweight.
  - [Bernstein · Gautam Chhugani / Mahika Sapra / Sanskar Chindalia / Harsh Misra — "AI Infra/miners: Texas data center audit to drive more MW scarcity" (2026-08-04)](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/Bernstein_WULF_AI_Inframiners_Texas_data_center_audit_to_drive_more_M.html) — **READ-THROUGH; subject is the ERCOT/Texas data-center audit and the bitcoin-miner / AI-infra cohort (WULF, CIFR, CORZ, CLSK, RIOT, IREN, MARA), not Amazon.** AWS-relevant content: Cipher Mining (CIFR) has 700 MW under construction for AWS and Fluidstack inside an approved 1.2 GW that is unaffected by the audit, but 2 GW of CIFR's 3.2 GW total pipeline needs ERCOT "Batch Zero" approval to energize and that review is now paused — so incremental AWS capacity from this counterparty beyond the contracted 700 MW is exposed to the delay.

(2026-05-27, HIST)](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/INTERNET_20260527_2032.html) — AWS/GCP '27 growth **36%/86% "achievable, potentially conservative"**; bottom-up ~14/20 GW incremental hyperscaler capacity '26/'27 (~6/8 GW to public cloud); **AMZN +3.5/~5 GW '26/'27** (GCP +3/3.5 GW); **~$14bn/$11bn incremental revenue per incremental GW '27 (AWS/GCP)** — the capacity-to-revenue bridge behind the AWS-reaccel case.

- **2026-09-09 (/wiki-ingest) — NOTHING SUPERSEDED.** 🔴 **NET-NEW: (1) AWS named as [[QCOM]]'s third custom-silicon customer, and the STRUCTURE is what matters on this page: Amazon received **25M Qualcomm warrants at an exercise price of $161.26/share**, vesting in tranches tied to **its own product and services purchases, up to a maximum of $60bn over 10 years** — roughly 2% of Qualcomm's equity, received for becoming a customer. The engagement spans **custom silicon AND high-performance optical connectivity ("starting with 1.6T, leveraging optical DSPs and, to a lesser degree, SerDes")**, expandable to merchant accelerators and CPU, and is worth **~$2bn of Qualcomm FY27 revenue**. This is the vendor-alignment mechanic the wiki tracks across the AI complex, run in REVERSE: the BUYER captures the equity upside of its own demand. Barclays adds the technical fit — "AWS has used QCOM's AI100 in their EC2 instances and uses PCIe SCALE UP so this fits" — and expects the ramp to be "back end loaded like what we saw with the [[MRVL]]/[[GOOG]] deal". (2) The best hard physical number on Amazon in this window, from Bernstein's August data-centre tracker: **Amazon contributed ~6GW of hyperscaler pipeline additions in the month and became THE FIRST OPERATOR TO EXCEED 10GW UNDER CONSTRUCTION** (industry under construction +4GW to ~70GW). The pipeline-quality context matters as much: the total project pipeline **surpassed 400GW (+23GW m/m to 404GW)** but only **~35%, or 170GW, is viewed as ultimately credible** — with **hyperscaler self-builds at the HIGHEST confidence level of 79% credibility (73GW)** versus colocation at 67% (55GW), and **stranded capacity up 7GW in August to a record 12% of the pipeline**. Amazon sits in the highest-credibility bucket of a pipeline two-thirds of which is judged not credible, which is the argument for owning the self-builder over the landlord.** ⚠️ **NOT ADOPTED: Bloomberg's "potentially constructing Trainium 3, shared with [[MRVL]] & Alchip" (unconfirmed, no split — it would mean three silicon partners on one programme); and EconDaily's report that AWS is increasing AI server-rack procurement, raising 3nm custom ASIC training and inference orders to [[TSM]] via Alchip with Wiwynn boosting rack/switch-tray production and Foxconn winning CPU-tray orders.** **ALSO LOGGED: [[GLW]]'s new Verizon fibre LTA explicitly cites Verizon as "the premier infrastructure partner for AWS"; AMZN Leo signed a partnership with AT&T; and Amazon launched a sterling debt offering on the day UK gilt yields hit a DMO record, read as US tech giants "increasingly diversifying into international bond markets".** Cross-filed [[QCOM]], [[MRVL]], [[ALAB]], [[GLW]], [[TSM]], [themes/hyperscaler-capex](themes/hyperscaler-capex.md), [themes/ai-datacenter-power](themes/ai-datacenter-power.md).

| **08-31/09-01** | 🔴🔴 **THE FTC AND 22 STATE AGs SUED AMAZON OVER THE ADVERTISING BUSINESS — ALLEGING BID MANIPULATION SINCE 2018 AND "TENS OF BILLIONS" OF OVERCHARGES** (GS · Eric Sheridan / Alex Vegliante / Sarah Boulos, 08-31 21:01 ET; The Information · Catherine Perloff; CNBC; FT; Reuters, 09-01) | **risco / receita** | **bear** | ★★ **THE FILING: *"On August 31st, the **US Federal Trade Commission along with 22 STATE ATTORNEYS GENERAL filed a lawsuit against Amazon** alleging that the company engaged in **deceptive and unfair practices related to its ADVERTISING business**"* (GS). **THE MECHANISM ALLEGED: *"Advertisers promoting their products on Amazon's marketplace buy ad spots where the price is set in auctions. In the complaint, the FTC and the states allege **Amazon ADDED AN EXTRA CHARGE TO BIDS in order to raise the price that advertisers paid ABOVE what one employee said could be achieved by 'ADVERTISER COMPETITION' alone.** The FTC also presented evidence that **Amazon took several steps to CONCEAL that it was inflating auction prices** from advertisers. The complaint says the practice **started in 2018 and CONTINUES TO THIS DAY**, and that it **likely resulted in advertisers overpaying by TENS OF BILLIONS**"* (The Information). **CNBC's sizing: *"the FTC sued AMZN alleging it **overcharged advertisers by $20B+ SINCE 2019** based on HIDDEN SURCHARGES with costs largely passed on to consumers"* (via MS desk).** ⚠️ **The two sources give different start years (2018 per The Information, "since 2019" per CNBC) and different quanta ("tens of billions" vs "$20B+"). Both logged as delivered; neither adopted.** 🔴 **AMAZON'S RESPONSE, VERBATIM AND FORCEFUL: the FTC's claim *"**fundamentally misunderstands how advertisers operate**"*, noting that *"**advertisers adjust bids based on real-world performance**"* and that *"**in NO SCENARIO does an advertiser pay MORE THAN THEIR BID**"*; Amazon also said it was *"**patently false**"* that it tried to deceive anyone. Separately: *"AMZN says the FTC's complaint over **Sponsored Ads** cites **no evidence of consumer price increases or advertiser harm**, and broadly this relates to **an area of the business that doesn't matter much to investors**"* (Amazon PR, via MS desk). ⚠️ **That last clause is Amazon's characterisation, not the desk's — and it is contestable: advertising is one of the highest-margin lines in the company.** 🔴 **THE PRECEDENT RISK IS THE PART TO WEIGH, AND IT IS SPECIFIC: *"The lawsuit is **REMINISCENT of antitrust cases against [[GOOG]]** which accused that company of also **MANIPULATING AD AUCTIONS**, in both its search advertising business and its advertising technology business. **GOOGLE LOST BOTH CASES.**"* And the docket context: *"the suit is **at least the THIRD lawsuit the FTC has filed against Amazon in the past few years**, including a major case alleging the tech giant **illegally monopolized ecommerce** — as part of that case the FTC called the advertising fees Amazon charges merchants '**MONOPOLY RENTS**'. **That case is expected to go to TRIAL NEXT YEAR.**"* Amazon settled a separate FTC case last year (the Prime dark-patterns case). ➤ ⚠️ **NO DAMAGES FIGURE, NO VENUE AND NO SCHEDULE for the new suit in any source as delivered — GS's note is a flag rather than an analysis (*"please see report for full details"* — the full report was not in the email body). OPEN ITEM: size the exposure against the ads revenue line on the next pass.** **Tape: AMZN −2.5% on 08-31, the biggest single-name drag on the hyperscaler basket that day (MSXXHYPR −1.7%).** |
| **09-01** | ✅🔴 **A REGULATORY WIN THAT CUTS THE OTHER WAY, AND IT MAY MATTER MORE STRATEGICALLY: THE AMAZON v PERPLEXITY RULING LIMITS AI-AGENT LIABILITY** (BofA · Michael McGovern, via spec sales Brian Fenske, 2026-09-01) | **produto / risco** | **bull** | ★ *"Mike McGovern sees the recent **Amazon v Perplexity ruling as an IMPORTANT EARLY PRECEDENT and potentially LIMITS AI AGENT LIABILITY. The second is the more durable of the two for the business model — agentic shopping is the channel-control question for retail, and "how marketplaces can detect and manage agentic traffic" is exactly the lever Amazon needs. ⚠️ BofA gives no case detail, court or date; the read-through is framed for SMID e-commerce rather than for AMZN itself.** **Also in the agentic-commerce file: *"YouTube's newest affiliate partner in YouTube shopping is AMZN — creators can now tag AMZN products in their YouTube content"* (Bloomberg via MS desk, 09-01) — a [[GOOG]]-AMZN distribution tie-up.** |

Amazon is not broken out in the 2025 split (AMD 35-40%, Intel mid-30s%, others 25-35%).** **③ THE FRAME THAT MATTERS FOR AWS's SILICON STRATEGY: Bernstein's thesis is that "AGENTIC AI WORKLOADS MAKE CPU THE RECENT SURPRISE TO TSMC," with TSMC's CPU revenue compounding 50-60% in 2026-27 to US$high-30s bn and CPU becoming "about as big as XPU in terms of WAFER revenue contribution in 2027." | ⏮ 06-29 (pub) / 06-15 (int.) | **AlphaSense expert call — Former Principal BD Manager, WW Generative AI at AWS (the same expert as the 03-12 and 07-16 calls already on this page; also ex-Global Head of GenAI Strategy at [[CEREBRAS]]). Interview 15 Jun 2026, published 29 Jun 2026; it PREDATES the Q2 print and the whole intra-quarter window. It arrived in the 08-07 team drop, not in the live flow. Every claim below is a Q2'26-vintage practitioner view, and the expert LEFT AWS IN FEBRUARY 2024 — so this is structural/process colour, not current-quarter information. It is logged because nothing else on this page describes the ALLOCATION MECHANICS between service tiers from inside.** 🔴 **THE MARGIN RANKING, UNAMBIGUOUS AND CONSISTENT WITH THE PAGE'S BEDROCK THESIS: asked which of EC2 / SageMaker / Bedrock creates the greatest stickiness at the highest margin — "THE HIGHEST MARGIN, IT'S IN BEDROCK. STICKINESS IS ALSO IN THE BEDROCK… The margin is highest in Bedrock because THEY ARE TAKING CONTROL OF THAT MODEL. When you as a customer are RELINQUISHING YOUR RIGHTS OR CONFIGURATIONS TO MANAGE… YOU ARE PAYING A PREMIUM. It's like a TIERED SERVICE… That's why from an AWS point of view, THEY WOULD PREFER TO HAVE CUSTOMERS SITTING IN THE BEDROCK ECOSYSTEM THAN INTO THE SAGEMAKER ECOSYSTEM."** ✅ **Independent practitioner corroboration of the SemiAnalysis Bedrock-margin teardown already on this page (2026-05-27) — same conclusion reached from operating experience rather than from financial reverse-engineering. NET-NEW quantification of the middle tier: SageMaker "charge[s] ABOUT 15% MORE THAN YOUR RAW COMPUTE," with customers spending "60%-70% LESS TIME in comparison to using raw compute" (raw EC2/Trainium "typically takes ONE WEEK TO 10 DAYS TO SPIN UP A MACHINE").** 🔴 **THE ALLOCATION MECHANICS, WHICH IS THE ROW'S REAL VALUE — and note it is an ESTIMATE, explicitly hedged by the expert: on how a new tranche of capacity gets split, "based on the current usage, based on the current footprint, PROBABLY 40%-50% WILL BE USED BY BEDROCK, 20% WILL BE USED BY SAGEMAKER, AND THE REMAINING… INTO THE AMAZON EC2 POOL. Again, IT IS JUST A ROUGH ESTIMATE based on my understanding."** ⚠️ **Do NOT treat that split as a disclosed mix — it is a former employee's recollection-based guess, two and a half years after leaving. The DEMAND-VALIDATION rule alongside it is the more durable claim: "90%-95% OF THE THING WHAT WE BUILD IS BASED ON THE NEEDS OF A CUSTOMER. If we do not have those needs VALIDATED BY CUSTOMERS OR BY THE NAMED ENTITIES, WE WOULD NOT EVEN BUILD IT. There are only 10%-15% of the things AWS builds which is NOT PARTICULARLY CALLED OUT BY A CUSTOMER… Without having that 80% CONFIDENCE, they would not go into that direction."** ⚠️ **The illustrative "out of 10,000 [Trainiums], probably 8,000 ARE GOING TO BE USED BY [[ANTHROPIC]]" is explicitly hypothetical — "I am just using the name here" — and must NOT be carried as an Anthropic allocation figure.** ✅ **PRICING DISCIPLINE UNDER SCARCITY — a direct answer to the "does AWS surcharge in a shortage" question, and it is a NO: "There is NO POINT IN AMAZON LIFE I HAVE SEEN WHERE WE WOULD CHARGE MORE CUSTOMERS BECAUSE OF THE LACK OF CAPACITY. We would DEFER that… 'we will give you 5,000 this week, and we will STAGGER IT'." Cost is agreed before capacity is allocated, via discount programmes.** ⚠️ **This CONTRADICTS the AWS "+20% fee hike on select GPU workloads effective July 1" that BofA reported on 2026-06-30 and that sits on this page. Both are retained: a list-price action on a SKU and a refusal to raise agreed prices on a committed customer under allocation are not the same thing, but the expert's "never" is stated more absolutely than the evidence supports.** 🔴 **THE INFERENCE-HARDWARE CLAIM THAT MATTERS TO [[NVDA]] AND IS THE MOST CONTESTABLE THING IN THE CALL: "WHEN WE ARE LOOKING AT INFERENCE AS A PROFILE, YOU DO NOT NEED THE HIGH-END GPU FOR INFERENCE… you can LIVE BY A100, WHICH WAS CREATED SIX YEARS AGO, AND IT WILL HAVE MORE OR LESS THE SAME LATENCY ON INFERENCE WHAT YOU HAD FOR H200 OR B200." Shortage framing to match: "IT IS NOT LIKE 2022, where everyone was fighting for GPU. Today, you can get a GPU… H100, EVEN B200 IN SOME CASES. A100 ARE AMPLE."** ⚠️⚠️ **NOT ADOPTED. It is flatly inconsistent with the memory-bandwidth-bound decode economics this wiki carries from [[NVDA]] and [tokenmaxxing](themes/tokenmaxxing.md) — where per-user interactivity scales with HBM bandwidth per token and generation-over-generation bandwidth gains are 2-3x. It is also a Q2-vintage view of a market that has tightened since. Logged as an attributed practitioner claim and flagged as the one item in this call a modelled view should reject.** ✅ **STRUCTURAL MOAT COLOUR, useful and consistent with the page: the security/sovereignty argument for going through a hyperscaler rather than direct to a lab ("typically 12-18 MONTHS to vet a cloud provider"; "Amazon will GUARANTEE THAT YOUR DATA WILL NEVER LEAVE AWS PERIMETER… a HARD GUARANTEE IN THE LEGAL CONTRACT"); the mechanic that makes it possible — "[[ANTHROPIC]] WILL HAND OFF THAT MODEL, THE BINARY, TO AWS… 'here is my Claude Opus. You run it for us on the GPU or Trainium'… [[ANTHROPIC]] IS ACTUALLY NOT IN THE LOOP, PER SE. It's PURELY AWS CONTROLLED AND MANAGED"; the enterprise-tooling gap at the labs ("[[OPENAI]] would not have this. Even Anthropic would not have this, because THEY ARE NOT YET INTO THE ENTERPRISE CUSTOMER MINDSET"); and data gravity as the durable lock ("if your data is in AWS, quite likely you will NOT MOVE THAT DATA… JUST BECAUSE THEY HAVE A BETTER MODEL, because NOW THE MODEL PARITY IS EVEN").** **Named references: Perplexity has trained on SageMaker "for almost three years now — ALL THE MODELS OF PERPLEXITY IS COMING FROM SAGEMAKER FACTORY"; BloombergGPT and the Falcon family trained by the expert's team; Bedrock scale given only as a range the expert would not commit to ("probably OVER 100,000 CUSTOMERS, MAYBE ONE MILLION… not the exact number I know of")** ⚠️ **— the page's disclosed Q1'26 figure of 125k+ Bedrock customers is the mark that stands; the expert's range is not used.** (AlphaSense expert call — Former Principal Business Development Manager, World Wide Generative AI, AWS; interview 2026-06-15, published 2026-06-29; received via the P: team drop 2026-08-07) [Source](../relat%C3%B3rios%20bons/AMZN_-_ExpertCall_document_dated_07-08-2026_7.html) |

Capacity: AMZN states "2026 CAPACITY WILL REMAIN INSUFFICIENT TO MEET DEMAND and that MUCH OF ITS 2027 CAPACITY IS ALREADY RESERVED."** **On ROIC — the most specific disclosure of the four: "AMZN emphasized that MOST AI CAPACITY IS CONTRACTED ON MULTI-YEAR TERMS, with SERVER INVESTMENTS ACHIEVING BREAKEVEN IN <3 YEARS and meaningful FCF generation thereafter, while mgmt now sees a path toward a SUBSTANTIALLY LARGER long-term AWS revenue opportunity."** ⚠️ **Counterweight already on this page, and it belongs alongside: Jassy's own late-binding language — "we typically purchase these A FEW MONTHS BEFORE putting them into service… IF THE DEMAND ISN'T THERE, WE WON'T SPEND THE CAPITAL." Model context: full modular on a 50 MW liquid-cooled hall = **~36% (7-9 months) faster, ~8% cheaper (~$13.5 vs ~$14.6mn/MW)**; an earlier month is worth **~$500k/MW** to an owner-operator (~$200mn / ~$4mn/MW on an ~8-month lead). Two datapoints beyond the AWS silicon thread: **DRAM is "the tightest supply chain constraint," with shortages expected to last until at least 2H2028** while **CPU supply is stable and not a bottleneck**; and **open-source plus Chinese models capture up to one-third of token usage**, concentrated at the low end, with frontier models keeping high-end, creative and enterprise work. Also notes **startups may accept slower inference to cut cost** — relevant to AWS's price-performance positioning against frontier-model APIs. Single expert, no financial disclosures — directional colour, not a datapoint. Cross-ref [NVDA.md](NVDA.md), [MU.md](MU.md). (AlphaSense expert call, published 2026-07-24; alert received 2026-07-25) |
| 08-18 | 🔴🔴 **MS · Brian Nowak (via TMT Sales · Skyler Risom) — "What's in $1T? AMZN AWS" — the road to a $1T AWS, and a $500 stock by YE27** | demanda / valuation / capital | **bull** | ★ **THE FULL BRIDGE FROM JASSY'S "$1T AWS" COMMENT TO A PRICE TARGET, WITH THE COMPUTE AND MONETISATION INPUTS NAMED.** MS's framing: *"Weakness in the AI complex and renewed skepticism over AI financing suggests that markets are overlooking the hard evidence of GenAI ROIC for Hyperscalers on display in 2Q earnings."* **The two Jassy quotes doing the work:** *"As we get a few years out and the revenue growth outpaces the incremental Capex growth, which will happen at some point, the resulting revenue, free cash flow, and return on invested capital is very compelling"*; and **AWS could hit $1T, with "margins and returns in AI" running "a little ahead" of this same point in the FIRST phase of the cloud-compute buildout.** 🔴 **THE COMPUTE INPUTS:** Nowak sees **AMZN bringing on 6GW of compute capacity in 2026 and 8GW in 2027**; on availability, **[[GOOG]] adds the most capacity (9GW / 11GW in 2027 / 2028) but AWS has the most AVAILABLE in 2028 — 35GW vs GOOGL at 31GW.** 🔴 **THE MONETISATION INPUT, and it is the single most transferable number in the note: "AWS is currently monetizing at $8/incremental watt, which Brian expects to grow: even just $12/watt would bring $1T revs in 2035."** ⚠️⚠️ **UNIT HAZARD — this is REVENUE per watt, not capex per watt. It must NOT be netted against $/W build-cost anchors or chained into a capex series.** **THE MARGIN BRIDGE:** management signals total AI business tracking toward *"similar margins + returns as Core"*, i.e. **~30% EBIT margins** → **~30% on $1T AWS revenue = ~$300B AWS EBIT around 2034-2036** → **adding Retail yields ~$500B EBIT in 2034-2036 (+16-20% CAGR)** → **$500B of long-term EBIT at 21x EV/EBIT = a $500 stock by YE27.** **Early investor feedback is that AWS margins could be HIGHER than 30%** — management *"sounded more bullish than usual on the call re AWS margins, citing 'disciplined efficiency gains' and 'capacity optimization.'"* ⚠️ **The one internal inconsistency MS itself flags and answers: why 30% AWS margins against 60-70% incremental EBIT margins in the GenAI ROIC framework? Because "Brian's broad Hyperscaler ROIC analysis uses leading-edge [[NVDA]] chips, whereas AWS compute mix includes lower-margin chips as well… though scaling towards GB300 can lift these levels higher toward the framework."** Under the Hyperscaler GPU-Rental (IaaS) model most relevant to AWS, MS models **60-70% incremental EBIT margins and >30% ROIC**, inside a broader **25-50% GenAI-investment ROIC** frame. |
| 08-18 | 🔴 **WSJ (via MS TMT Sales) — $3T of off-balance-sheet AI commitments across the hyperscalers** | capital | **bear** | ★ **The counterweight to the row above, and it is the same desk carrying both.** WSJ reports that **AMZN, [[GOOG]], [[META]] and others have ~$3T in off-balance-sheet commitments, mostly AI-related, versus traditional capex of ~$600B over the past year** — i.e. the disclosed capex line understates committed spend by roughly 5x. MS's own accounting team (**Todd Castagno / Lindsay Tyler**) sized this exposure in detail back in April. 🔴 **Why it matters specifically for the $500 bridge above: the MS valuation runs off long-term EBIT at 21x, which does not obviously charge for lease-and-power obligations that sit outside capex — the same critique BofA has now formalised on [[NVDA]] by discounting "FCF servicing customer financing" by 50%. Both frameworks are on the wiki as of 08-18 and they are NOT reconciled: one hyperscaler analyst capitalises the EBIT at a full multiple while a semis analyst haircuts the financing-linked cash flow by half. That gap is the alpha, and it is deliberately left open.** **Tape context:** Internet names fell 1% on 08-17 with **AMZN −4%** (MS Internet, 08-18); AMZN carried a **5-day absolute negative streak and a 5-day relative underperformance streak vs QQQ** (Barclays desk, 08-18); AMZN/GOOGL/META trade at **20x/17x/19x '26 EPS = −35%/−35%/−19% vs TTM average.** **The desk-level frustration, verbatim from Jefferies (08-18): "hyperscalers and the check spenders lag the near-term AI beneficiaries even after putting up phenomenal results and answering many of the ROIC questions."** |
| 08-15 | All-In Podcast Ep. #285 — Calacanis vs Sacks (equity-calls archive; secondary read-through) | regulacao | neutral | **Logged for completeness, low investment weight.** Calacanis argues Amazon should proactively internalise more **delivery-service-partner (DSP)** drivers *"to protect capitalism's legitimacy and improve benefits"*; **Sacks defends the contractor model and cites a study claiming forced reclassification would add $5.20 per package / $664 per NYC household per year and risk ~5,000 jobs.** ⚠️ **The study is not identified in the episode, so those figures are unverified.** No numbers change on this page. |

**Quarter synthesis:** the debate migrated from "is AWS reaccelerating?" (already answered: +28%, silicon validated, margin inflecting) to the sustainability of the funding and the health of the non-AI core — JPM shows FCF collapsing while AMZN pre-funds ~$85bn of the FY27 ramp, and the only crack in the bull is the non-AI cloud SSO weakness that Bernstein sees threatening 4Q. **06-29: BofA Justin Post confirms AWS compute pricing power** — +20% CB price hike (July 1), effective prices +22% from 2022 trough, adds 1–2ppt to 2H AWS growth. Prime Day +9% ($26.4B) confirms consumer demand resilience. **06-30: BofA flags a Q3-guide risk from the Prime Day July→June shift (~$7-8bn out of Q3), and R&Co reports Anthropic renegotiating how Amazon pays to use its models next year — the first crack of pricing-power tension in the marquee AWS-Anthropic relationship.** Separately, deep-dive silicon research (SemiAnalysis CPU + HBM reports, folded in 06-30) broadens the custom-silicon validation beyond Trainium alone: Graviton5 (192 cores/172B transistors) is now wired directly into the Trainium3 ramp as head nodes, and both the CPU (PCIe lanes cut 96→64) and accelerator (Trainium2e's HBM3E sourcing) sides show the same deliberate cost-over-peak-spec philosophy — a coherent, distinctive positioning point for the AWS silicon thesis, alongside Barron's corroborating the $168B AWS 2026 revenue and $215B 2-yr capex scale. **06-30/07-01 update: the AWS-pricing-power and silicon-demand confirms keep piling up (BofA's +20% GPU-workload fee hike restated with more color on Bedrock/OpenAI/Anthropic drivers; UBS lifts Trainium3 unit demand to 1.8mn/2.8mn '26E/'27E; MS models AMZN's 2027 IT-capacity build at ~5GW, ahead of META but behind GOOGL; Barclays ties AMZN into the new "Meta Compute" external-cloud story) — but two new bear-relevant threads emerged in parallel: (1) valuation/sentiment is lagging the operational print (MS: AMZN -5% in a month, now 25x '26 EPS / -22% vs TTM avg; JPM's sales desk lists a widening bear overhang sheet — weak-consumer risk, higher '26/'27 capex ests on component pricing, neocloud competition, possible equity issuance, and the "Leo" satellite push); (2) the Anthropic relationship, the single largest AI-customer concentration in the AWS backlog, now has a concrete repricing mechanism attached (token-based billing replacing compute-hours starting next year) with Amazon reportedly already hedging via model distillation and in-house Nova evaluation — sharpening R&Co's 06-30 "pricing power" flag into a named economic mechanism. AWS is also visibly expanding the moat further into services (R&Co Redburn: $1bn Forward Deployed Engineering unit, Palantir-style, 07-01) — bullish for AWS TAM breadth, bearish for IT-services comps. **07-07: GS (Sheridan) puts a bow on the AWS-acceleration long ahead of the Q2 print — Buy, PT raised $325→$335, AWS growth lifted to ~33%/~35% '26/'27, 2027-30 AWS capex raised (~$827B '26-'28, $50-60B/GW build cost, FCFE negative in '27), Trainium framed as a forward-margin lever, and the consumer read (Prime Day) stable-but-price-sensitive — the most complete single-note statement of the four investor debates (consumer / capex-ROIC / AWS growth / custom silicon) the desk flow has been circling all quarter.** **07-08: BofA (Fenske) puts AMZN on the TMT ideas-call Long list (with GOOGL, the semicaps, and TXN/ADI) — desk positioning turning constructive into the print even as the stock de-rates; Susquehanna (Patil) hosting a Jun/Jul "Search and YouTube Trends" expert call flagged for GOOGL/AMZN keeps the ad/search-demand read-through live ahead of Q2.** **07-11/12: the cross-hyperscaler capex notes both land — Mizuho pegs hyperscaler-only capex at $846B/$912B '27/'28 (up 54%/66% off YE-25 cons) with sector FCF dipping to $68B/$61B '26/'27E before inflecting in '28, and MS lifts AMZN company-wide capex to $308bn/$318bn while modeling AWS with the most available compute of any hyperscaler by '28 (~35 GW) — both reinforce the AWS-capacity-leadership + capex-up narrative into the print.** **07-12: BofA's buyside bogey poll (Fenske) shows the reaccel long has crossed to the buyside base case — AWS bogey +34.3% Q2 → +35.7% Q3 (reported), slightly above GS — while the buyside capex bogeys ($204.5bn'26/$277.7bn'27, ramping to $306.6bn'28/$326.1bn'29) sit below the top-end company-wide sell-side capex (MS $308bn'27, different basis), i.e. the buyside is underwriting the AWS growth without yet marking the most aggressive capex; buyside Q2/Q3 GAAP EBIT $24.7bn/$25.6bn and 2026/27 GAAP EBIT $108.2bn/$141.0bn.** **07-17/07-20 (into the print): Barclays (Sandler) reiterates OW/$330 with the cleanest statement of the margin-agnostic-hyperscaler thesis — Bedrock earns ~35% OI margin whether it serves Anthropic Sonnet 5 or open-source Kimi 2.5, so token fragmentation doesn't dent AWS margin — while Rothschild/Redburn (Haissl/Han) opens the first hard margin short of the quarter (Neutral, Street-low PT $230: AWS gross margin down 520bp Q4-24→Q1-26, Gen-AI-only book margin ~12%, "strongest margins on thinnest disclosure"). On capital, JPM's joint equity-&-credit call quantifies the funding turn (+42% capex to ~$300bn '27, $92bn raised YTD across 4 currencies, the $25bn Jul-7 issuance widening its own bonds ~20bp, an S&P downgrade trigger at 1.5x net leverage) while keeping AMZN the equity top pick; BofA's Big-Tech-setups round frames the print at 2Q rev ~$198bn / AWS 33-34% (Yipit 35-36%), Anthropic >$2bn of Amazon revenue this quarter and Trainium backlog +$100bn q/q.** **07-22 (BofA 2Q preview into the 07-30 print):** Post reiterates Buy/$310 and models a beat ($198.8bn/$24.1bn vs Street $196.8bn/$23.6bn), RAISES BofA's own AWS growth est to 33% (from 31%) with 3Q accelerating to 36% y/y, flags a possible 2026 capex-outlook raise to $210bn (and '27 to $282bn / $230bn AWS) on memory, and carries $100bn of Anthropic backlog in 2Q — the dedicated preview restating the AWS-reaccel long into the print. **07-26 (read-through, UBS MSFT pre-print note — the last independent demand check before the 7/30 print):** UBS's ~dozen customer/partner conversations describe **"Azure/AWS demand… very healthy"** with cloud-infra checks "very strong over the last several quarters (and indeed this has shown up in the sequential dollar adds of AWS, Azure, and Google Cloud)," hedged as "still healthy albeit with some pockets of caution" — and UBS tells clients outright to trade AWS off the MSFT 7/29 call ("including as a read to AWS"). Two items are net-new to this page. First, a fourth independent house now states the **front-end monetization mechanic** that underwrites the Bedrock margin story: AWS, Azure and Google Cloud "**recognize revenues**" from selling access to OpenAI/Anthropic models and their **1P products "such as Claude Code"** — "a source of revenue upside that goes well beyond serving as providers of compute infrastructure" — with the symmetric warning that open-model routing "could impact the growth of these revenue streams in 2H," and one AWS-specific offset (customers funding token overruns by "optimizing elsewhere in their Azure/AWS spend"). Second, and it lands squarely on the quarter's live margin fight: **UBS contests Redburn's Street-low $230 premise directly** — "we've made the same observation about the margin performance at **AWS, Oracle** and others — on the surface, **AI capex/revs are not landing at the abysmal margin levels that the bears feared**," evidenced on MSFT by gross margin falling only 110bps y/y on $37bn (11% of revs) of AI revenue. Both sides are now on the page unresolved. Field colour: one customer runs **~80% AWS / rest Azure with cloud spend +25% y/y, the marquee driver being SAP-to-RISE "in AWS"**; the counter-anecdote is a customer cutting Azure 5-10% and going to **Google**, not AWS. Positioning marks from the same note's peer table (7/24/26): **AMZN +1% YTD is the best performer of the twelve names (mean -33%) and carries the set's highest non-GAAP P/E at 23.9x/21.4x CY26E/CY27E on +15.0%/+13.4% revenue growth** — i.e. into the print AMZN is the consensus AI-infra long being paid for, not the value option.
